<tr><td>↳近 distinction</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋkʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>殊荣，卓越；区别</td><td>a mark of distinction<br>卓越的标志</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He graduated with distinction from a small rural college.  他以优异成绩从一所乡下的小型学院毕业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 endorsement</td><td>/ɪnˈdɔːrsmənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>认可，赞同；代言</td><td>a public endorsement<br>公开的支持认可</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidate won the endorsement of two major unions.  这位候选人获得两大工会的支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 homage</td><td>/ˈhɑːmɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>尊敬，敬意</td><td>pay homage to<br>向…致敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands came to pay homage to the poet on her ninetieth birthday.  数千人在诗人九十岁生日那天前来致敬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 honor</td><td>/ˈɑːnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>尊敬，给以荣誉；荣誉</td><td>honor the veterans<br>表彰老兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city honored the veterans with a quiet morning ceremony.  该市用一场安静的晨间仪式表彰老兵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kudos</td><td>/ˈkuːdoʊz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>名望，名声；夸奖，赞扬</td><td>kudos for the win<br>因获胜而受赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She earned kudos from critics for her fearless debut novel.  她凭无畏的处女作小说赢得了评论界的赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disbelief</td><td>/ˌdɪsbɪˈliːf/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不信，怀疑</td><td>stare in disbelief<br>难以置信地盯着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She listened to the verdict with open disbelief.  她听着判决，一脸难以置信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 doubt</td><td>/ˈdaʊt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>怀疑，疑虑</td><td>cast doubt on<br>使人怀疑…</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is little doubt that the data was carelessly recorded.  几乎可以肯定，这些数据记录得很草率。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 dubiety</del></td><td>/duˈbaɪəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>怀疑，疑惑</td><td>a note of dubiety<br>一丝怀疑的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A trace of dubiety showed in the professor's careful reply.  教授谨慎的回答中透出一丝怀疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 incertitude</del></td><td>/ɪnˈsɜːrtɪtuːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不确定，疑虑</td><td>a state of incertitude<br>疑虑不定的状态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Months of incertitude about funding paralyzed the whole project.  数月对资金的不确定使整个项目停滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 denunciation</td><td>/dɪˌnʌnsiˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谴责，公开指责</td><td>a public denunciation<br>公开的谴责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her speech was a fierce denunciation of the new policy.  她的演讲是对新政策的猛烈谴责。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 excoriation</td><td>/ɪkˌskɔːriˈeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>严厉指责，痛斥</td><td>public excoriation<br>公开的痛斥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editorial was an excoriation of the mayor's housing policy.  这篇社论是对市长住房政策的痛斥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rebuke</td><td>/rɪˈbjuːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>斥责，责难</td><td>a sharp rebuke<br>严厉的斥责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge rebuked the lawyer for interrupting the witness.  法官斥责律师打断证人发言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reprimand</td><td>/ˈreprəˌmænd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>训斥，正式谴责</td><td>reprimand an employee<br>训斥员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The officer was reprimanded for leaving his post early.  这名警官因提早离岗而受到训斥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reproach</td><td>/rɪˈproʊtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>批评，责备</td><td>reproach oneself for<br>因某事自责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She reproached him for never returning her calls.  她责备他从不回她的电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reproof</td><td>/rɪˈpruːf/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>批评，责备</td><td>a gentle reproof<br>温和的责备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single glance from her served as a silent reproof.  她的一个眼神就成了无声的责备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stricture</td><td>/ˈstrɪktʃər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>责难，批评</td><td>harsh strictures on<br>对…的严厉批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The review contained harsh strictures on the author's careless research.  评论对作者草率的研究提出严厉批评。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>crow</b></td><td>/kroʊ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>得意地夸耀，自鸣得意</td><td>crow about the win<br>为获胜而得意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He crowed about his victory until even his closest friends grew tired.  他为胜利得意个不停，连最亲近的朋友都听厌了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delight</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使高兴，使愉快；愉悦</td><td>delight the audience<br>使观众高兴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist delighted the audience with a lively encore.  这位年轻钢琴家用一首活泼的加演曲取悦了观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 exuberate</del></td><td>/ɪɡˈzubəˌreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>兴高采烈，极其欢欣</td><td>exuberate over the news<br>为这消息欢欣不已</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole village exuberated when the harvest proved unusually rich.  收成出奇地好，全村人欢天喜地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glory</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>感到自豪，得意；荣耀</td><td>glory in her success<br>为她的成功而得意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gloried in the praise his research finally received.  他为研究终于获得的赞誉而洋洋自得。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jubilate</td><td>/ˈdʒubəleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>欢欣，喜悦</td><td>jubilate over the news<br>为这消息而欢欣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole town began to jubilate when the rescue team returned safely.  救援队平安归来时，全城开始欢庆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 joy</td><td>/ˈdʒɔɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欢乐，喜悦</td><td>jump for joy<br>高兴得跳起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The news of his recovery filled the whole family with joy.  他康复的消息让全家人充满喜悦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rejoice</td><td>/rɪˈdʒɔɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>欣喜，喜悦</td><td>rejoice at the news<br>为消息而欣喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole village rejoiced at the news of the safe return.  听到平安归来的消息，全村都欣喜不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 triumph</td><td>/ˈtraɪəmf/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>巨大成功，胜利</td><td>a stunning military triumph<br>惊人的军事胜利</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new bridge is a triumph of modern engineering and careful planning.  这座新桥是现代工程与周密规划的巨大成功。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bemoan</td><td>/bɪˈmoʊn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>哀叹，抱怨</td><td>bemoan his bad luck<br>哀叹自己运气不好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farmers bemoaned the loss of their crops after the flood.  洪水过后农民哀叹作物尽毁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 bewail</del></td><td>/bɪˈweɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>痛惜，哀叹</td><td>bewail her sad fate<br>哀叹她的悲惨命运</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers gathered to bewail the loss of their homes.  村民聚在一起痛惜失去家园。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grieve</td><td>/ɡriːv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>（使）感到悲伤</td><td>grieve for a lost friend<br>为逝去的朋友悲伤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole village grieved for the fishermen lost in the autumn storm.  全村人都为在秋季风暴中失踪的渔民而悲伤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lament</td><td>/ləˈment/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>为…哀悼，表达痛苦或遗憾</td><td>lament the loss<br>哀叹损失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Older residents still lament the loss of the orchards that once surrounded town.  年长的居民仍在哀叹曾环绕小镇的果园的消失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weep</td><td>/ˈwiːp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>哭泣，流泪</td><td>weep bitterly<br>痛哭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some viewers still weep at the closing scene of that old film.  有些观众看到那部老电影的结尾时依然会哭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>damn</b></td><td>/dæm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（在道德上）谴责</td><td>damn the whole practice<br>谴责整套做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics damned the novel as cruel and morally irresponsible.  批评者谴责这部小说残忍且在道德上不负责任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 anathematize</del></td><td>/əˈnæθəmətaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诅咒，痛斥</td><td>anathematize the heretics<br>诅咒异端者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council met to anathematize anyone denying the doctrine.  会议召开是为了诅咒任何否认该教义的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 censure</td><td>/ˈsenʃər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>公开表示反对，谴责</td><td>a vote of censure<br>谴责性投票</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council voted to censure the mayor for his conduct.  议会投票谴责市长的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decry</td><td>/dɪˈkraɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈反对，否定</td><td>decry the new policy<br>强烈反对新政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Local doctors decried the plan to close the only children's clinic.  当地医生强烈反对关闭唯一一家儿童诊所的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 denounce</td><td>/dɪˈnaʊns/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开指责</td><td>denounce corruption<br>公开指责腐败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Local leaders publicly denounced the corruption exposed by the newspaper.  当地领导人公开谴责报纸曝光的腐败行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 execrate</del></td><td>/ˈeksɪkreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>痛恨，咒骂</td><td>execrate a tyrant<br>痛斥暴君</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Historians still execrate the tyrant for his cruelty.  史学家仍因其残暴而痛斥这个暴君。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 reprehend</del></td><td>/reprɪˈhend/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谴责，指责</td><td>reprehend such conduct<br>谴责这种行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee did not hesitate to reprehend the officer for his careless report.  委员会毫不犹豫地谴责了那名官员草率的报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprobate</td><td>/ˈreprəbeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>堕落者，道德败坏的人</td><td>an old reprobate<br>一个老无赖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel's hero is a charming reprobate with no money.  小说的主角是个迷人却身无分文的堕落者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bless</td><td>/ˈbles/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，祝福</td><td>bless the harvest<br>为收成祝福</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The priest blessed the fields at the start of spring.  神父在春天开始时为田地祝福。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eulogize</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>称赞；颂扬</td><td>eulogize the late leader<br>颂扬已故领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues gathered to eulogize the scientist who had trained them all.  同事们聚在一起颂扬这位培养了他们所有人的科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exalt</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞扬；提升（地位）</td><td>exalt to the skies<br>推崇到极点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics exalt her early novels as masterpieces of the era.  评论家将她早期的小说赞为那个时代的杰作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extol</td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glorify</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːrəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，美化</td><td>glorify war and violence<br>美化战争与暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics said the film glorified war instead of questioning it.  批评者说这部电影美化战争而非质疑它。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dampen</b></td><td>/ˈdæmpən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抑制，压抑（感情、精力等），泼冷水</td><td>dampen his enthusiasm<br>打击他的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Losing the first game did nothing to dampen the team's spirits.  首场失利丝毫没有打击球队的士气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 benumb</del></td><td>/bɪˈnʌm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使麻木，使迟钝</td><td>benumb the fingers<br>使手指麻木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The freezing wind benumbed our fingers within a few short minutes.  刺骨寒风几分钟就让我们的手指麻木了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blunt</td><td>/blʌnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>直言不讳的；钝的</td><td>a blunt refusal<br>直率的拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her blunt refusal left no room for further discussion.  她直率的拒绝没给进一步讨论留下余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 castrate</td><td>/ˈkæsˌtreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阉割；使失去活力</td><td>castrate the argument<br>削弱论点的力量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Editors castrated the essay by cutting its sharpest passages.  编辑删去最犀利的段落，使文章失去了力量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deaden</td><td>/ˈdedən/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使减弱，使麻木</td><td>deaden the pain<br>减轻疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick carpets deaden the sound of footsteps upstairs.  厚地毯减弱了楼上的脚步声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 devitalize</del></td><td>/diːˈvaɪtəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使失去活力</td><td>devitalize the local economy<br>使当地经济失去活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of neglect devitalized the once thriving downtown district.  多年的忽视使曾经繁荣的市中心失去活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 enervate</del></td><td>/ˈenərveɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使衰弱</td><td>enervate the whole team<br>使全队疲弱无力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long heat wave enervated the whole team before the final match.  漫长的热浪在决赛前使全队疲弱无力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 geld</td><td>/ɡɛld/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>削弱，使无力；阉割</td><td>geld the original bill<br>削弱原议案的力度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Endless amendments gelded the reform until it had no force left.  无休止的修正案把这项改革削弱得毫无力量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 lobotomize</del></td><td>/ləˈbɑtəmaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>切除脑白质；使麻木呆滞</td><td>lobotomize the audience<br>使观众变得麻木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hours of mindless television seemed to lobotomize the whole family.  几小时无脑电视似乎让全家人都变得麻木呆滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 petrify</td><td>/ˈpetrɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使僵化，使失去活力</td><td>petrify a living tradition<br>使活的传统僵化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Endless rules can petrify a living tradition into empty ritual.  无穷的规矩会使活的传统僵化为空洞的仪式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brace</td><td>/ˈbreɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使振作，鼓励；支撑</td><td>brace yourself for impact<br>做好承受冲击准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cup of hot tea braced him for the long night.  一杯热茶让他为漫长的夜晚振作起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 energize</td><td>/ˈenərdʒaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使充满活力，激励</td><td>energize the whole team<br>使整个团队振奋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new coach energized a team that had lost hope.  新教练让失去希望的球队重新振奋起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enliven</td><td>/ɪnˈlaɪvn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使活跃，使生动</td><td>enliven a dull party<br>让沉闷的聚会活跃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her jokes enlivened an otherwise dull afternoon meeting.  她的玩笑让本来沉闷的下午会议活跃起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 invigorate</td><td>/ɪnˈvɪɡəreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使精神，使强壮，鼓舞激励</td><td>invigorate the local economy<br>振兴当地经济</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A short walk in cold air always invigorates my tired mind.  在冷空气中短暂散步总能让我疲惫的头脑重新振作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quicken</td><td>/ˈkwɪkən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>加快；使有生气，激活</td><td>quicken the pace<br>加快步伐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The good news quickened her pulse and lifted her spirits.  好消息让她心跳加快，精神振奋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stimulate</td><td>/ˈstɪmjəˌleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，激发；促进</td><td>stimulate economic growth<br>促进经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm rain stimulated the seeds to sprout within a week.  温暖的雨水刺激种子一周内发芽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vitalize</td><td>/ˈvaɪtəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赋予活力，激励</td><td>vitalize the local economy<br>使当地经济充满活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New investment vitalized the sleepy town within just two years.  新投资在两年内使这个沉寂的小镇焕发活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 vivify</del></td><td>/ˈvɪvɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使生动，赋予生命</td><td>vivify a dull narrative<br>使乏味的叙述生动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vivid dialogue vivifies an otherwise dull historical narrative.  生动的对话使原本乏味的历史叙述活了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dashing</b></td><td>/ˈdæʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>衣着讲究的，潇洒时髦的</td><td>a dashing young officer<br>潇洒的年轻军官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dashing young officer looked immaculate in his dress uniform.  那位潇洒的年轻军官穿着礼服显得一丝不乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adventurous</td><td>/ədˈventʃərəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱冒险的，大胆的</td><td>an adventurous traveler<br>爱冒险的旅行者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their adventurous son plans to cycle across the whole continent.  他们爱冒险的儿子打算骑车横穿整个大陆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 audacious</td><td>/ɔːˈdeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，（但往往）愚勇的；令人吃惊的</td><td>an audacious plan<br>大胆的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general approved an audacious plan to cross the river at night.  将军批准了一个夜间渡河的大胆计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 daring</td><td>/ˈderɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>勇敢，大胆</td><td>an act of daring<br>大胆之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Climbing that face alone took real daring and long preparation.  独自攀那面岩壁需要真正的胆量和长期准备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emboldened</td><td>/ɪmˈboʊldənd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>受到鼓舞的，胆子大起来的</td><td>emboldened by early success<br>因初期成功而胆壮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Emboldened by the applause, she read a second poem.  掌声让她胆子大了起来，又读了第二首诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enterprising</td><td>/ˈentərpraɪzɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有进取心的，有魄力的</td><td>an enterprising young reporter<br>有闯劲的年轻记者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some enterprising students started a delivery service on campus.  几个有闯劲的学生在校园里办起了配送服务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gutsy</td><td>/ˈɡʌtsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有勇气的，大胆的</td><td>a gutsy performance<br>有勇气的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a gutsy move to bet everything on one project.  把一切押在一个项目上是很有勇气的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nerved</td><td>/ˈnɜːrvd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>鼓起勇气的，被激励的</td><td>nerved for the attempt<br>为尝试鼓足了勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nerved by his friend's example, he finally spoke up.  受朋友榜样激励，他终于开口了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nervy</td><td>/ˈnɜːrvi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧张不安的；大胆无礼的</td><td>feel nervy<br>感到紧张不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The horse grew nervy as the crowd pressed closer.  人群靠近时，那匹马变得紧张不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 venturous</del></td><td>/ˈvɛntʃərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，敢于冒险的</td><td>a venturous explorer<br>敢于冒险的探险家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only the most venturous sailors tried that northern route.  只有最敢冒险的水手才尝试那条北方航线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cowardly</td><td>/ˈkaʊərdli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>怯懦的，胆小的</td><td>a cowardly attack<br>卑怯的袭击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was cowardly to blame the mistake on his assistant.  把错误推给助手是怯懦的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 craven</td><td>/ˈkreɪvn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常懦弱的，因胆小而遭人鄙视的</td><td>a craven retreat<br>怯懦的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His craven silence during the trial disappointed everyone who had trusted him.  他在庭审中懦弱的沉默让所有信任过他的人失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pusillanimous</td><td>/ˌpjuːsɪˈlænɪməs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小的，懦弱的</td><td>a pusillanimous response<br>软弱的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the government's pusillanimous silence a betrayal of victims.  批评者称政府懦弱的沉默是对受害者的背叛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 timid</td><td>/ˈtɪmɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆怯的，羞怯的</td><td>a timid smile<br>怯生生的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a timid smile and slipped into the back row.  她怯生生地一笑，溜进后排座位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 timorous</td><td>/ˈtɪmərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小的</td><td>a timorous little voice<br>胆怯的小声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The timorous student finally raised her hand to answer the question.  那个胆怯的学生终于举手回答问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dated</b></td><td>/ˈdeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的</td><td>a dated hairstyle<br>过时的发型</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The furniture looked hopelessly dated beside the building's new glass walls.  在大楼新的玻璃幕墙旁，这些家具显得过时至极。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antiquated</td><td>/ˈæntɪkweɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古老的，过时的</td><td>an antiquated system<br>过时的系统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office still runs on an antiquated system built in the eighties.  办公室仍在使用八十年代建成的过时系统。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 archaic</td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，久远的</td><td>archaic legal language<br>古旧的法律用语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract still uses archaic terms that nobody understands.  这份合同仍在使用无人理解的古旧术语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fossilized</td><td>/ˈfɑːsəˌlaɪzd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>僵化的，化石化的</td><td>fossilized attitudes<br>僵化的观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The department's fossilized rules discouraged every new hire.  部门僵化的规定让每个新人失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 medieval</td><td>/mɪˈdiːvəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>中世纪的；过时的</td><td>medieval attitudes toward women<br>对女性的陈腐观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company's leave policy feels frankly medieval today.  这家公司的休假制度如今显得实在过时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moribund</td><td>/ˈmɔːrɪbʌnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>垂死的，即将消亡的</td><td>a moribund industry<br>垂死的行业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap imports left the region's textile industry moribund within a single decade.  廉价进口品在十年内就让该地区的纺织业濒于消亡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moth-eaten</td><td>/ˈmɔːθ ˌiːtən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虫蛀的；陈旧过时的</td><td>a moth-eaten coat<br>被虫蛀的外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a moth-eaten sweater from his college days.  他穿着大学时代那件被虫蛀的毛衣来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obsolete</td><td>/ˌɑːbsəˈliːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，被淘汰的</td><td>become obsolete<br>变得过时</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Once a marvel, the machine became obsolete within five years of its release.  这台机器曾是奇迹，上市五年内就被淘汰了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outdated</td><td>/ˈaʊtˌdeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，陈旧的</td><td>outdated equipment<br>过时的设备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their outdated software could not handle the new files.  他们过时的软件无法处理新文件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outmoded</td><td>/ˌaʊtˈmoʊdɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，废弃的</td><td>outmoded ideas<br>过时的观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory still relies on outmoded machinery from the nineteen sixties.  这家工厂仍在使用二十世纪六十年代的过时机器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 outworn</del></td><td>/ˌaʊtˈwɔrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧的，过时的</td><td>outworn traditions<br>过时的传统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She quietly abandoned the outworn methods of her teachers.  她悄悄抛弃了老师们过时的方法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prehistoric</td><td>/ˌpriːhɪˈstɔːrɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>史前的；极古老的</td><td>prehistoric cave paintings<br>史前洞穴壁画</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Archaeologists found prehistoric tools beside the dried riverbed.  考古学家在干涸的河床旁发现了史前工具。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rusty</td><td>/ˈrʌsti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>声音生涩沙哑的</td><td>a rusty voice<br>生涩的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After years of silence his voice sounded rusty and thin.  多年不语后，他的声音听起来生涩而单薄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 contemporary</td><td>/kənˈtempəˌreri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>当代的；同时代的</td><td>contemporary art<br>当代艺术</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallery mixes ancient sculpture with contemporary photography.  这家画廊将古代雕塑与当代摄影混合展出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 current</td><td>/ˈkɜːrənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>现在的，通行的；水流，电流</td><td>current practice<br>现行做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The current version of the software fixes several old bugs.  该软件的现行版本修复了若干旧漏洞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modern</td><td>/ˈmɑːdərn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>现代的，新式的</td><td>modern architecture<br>现代建筑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Modern phones can do far more than early computers could.  现代手机的功能远超早期的计算机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 recent</td><td>/ˈriːsənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>最近的，近来的</td><td>in recent years<br>近几年来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Recent studies suggest that short naps improve memory.  最近的研究表明小睡能改善记忆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>deadlock</b></td><td>/ˈdedlɑːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>僵局；使陷入僵局，使停顿</td><td>break the political deadlock<br>打破政治僵局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The talks reached a deadlock over the question of foreign inspectors.  谈判在外国检查员的问题上陷入僵局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gridlock</td><td>/ˈɡrɪdˌlɑːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>僵局；交通瘫痪</td><td>political gridlock in congress<br>国会中的政治僵局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Budget talks ended in complete gridlock again this spring.  预算谈判今春又一次陷入彻底僵局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 halt</td><td>/ˈhɔːlt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>停止，暂停</td><td>come to a halt<br>停下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow brought the whole city to a halt.  大雪使整座城市陷入停滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impasse</td><td>/ˈɪmpæs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>僵局，绝境</td><td>reach an impasse<br>陷入僵局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The talks reached an impasse over pay and working hours.  谈判在薪酬和工时上陷入僵局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 logjam</td><td>/ˈlɑːɡˌdʒæm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>僵局，停滞</td><td>break the legislative logjam<br>打破立法僵局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single compromise finally broke the logjam over pensions.  一项妥协终于打破了养老金问题的僵局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stalemate</td><td>/ˈsteɪlˌmeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>僵局，和棋</td><td>reach a stalemate<br>陷入僵局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two sides reached a stalemate after nine hours of talks.  谈了九个小时后双方陷入僵局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 standoff</td><td>/ˈstænˌdɔːf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>僵持，对峙</td><td>a tense armed standoff<br>紧张的武装对峙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The standoff outside the embassy lasted three long days.  使馆外的对峙持续了漫长的三天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 standstill</td><td>/ˈstændˌstɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>停顿，停滞</td><td>come to a standstill<br>陷入停顿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic came to a complete standstill after the accident.  事故发生后交通完全陷入停顿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expedite</td><td>/ˈekspədaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加快进程</td><td>expedite the process<br>加快流程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hiring two more clerks expedited the review of every application.  又雇了两名职员加快了每份申请的审核。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>debonair</b></td><td>/ˌdebəˈner/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>风度翩翩的，温文尔雅的</td><td>a debonair manner<br>从容优雅的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debonair gentleman charmed every woman in the crowded ballroom.  这位风度翩翩的绅士让拥挤舞厅里的每位女士都为之倾倒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blithe</td><td>/blaɪð/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愉快高兴的；无忧无虑的；漫不经心的</td><td>a blithe disregard<br>漫不经心的无视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His blithe disregard for the rules annoyed the older staff.  他对规则漫不经心的无视让资深员工不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 carefree</td><td>/ˈkerfriː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无忧无虑的</td><td>a carefree summer<br>无忧无虑的夏天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a carefree summer swimming in the lake every day.  我们度过了一个无忧无虑的夏天，每天在湖里游泳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gay</td><td>/ˈɡeɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>快乐的，欢快的</td><td>gay laughter<br>欢快的笑声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Gay music and bright lanterns filled the village square that evening.  那晚欢快的音乐和明亮的灯笼充满了村中广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insouciant</td><td>/ɪnˈsuːsiənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无忧虑的，不在乎的</td><td>an insouciant shrug<br>满不在乎地耸肩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the harsh criticism with an insouciant shrug and a smile.  他对严厉的批评只是满不在乎地耸耸肩，笑了笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lighthearted</td><td>/ˌlaɪtˈhɑːrtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻松愉快的，无忧无虑的</td><td>a lighthearted comedy<br>轻松的喜剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We wanted a lighthearted film after such a stressful week.  在如此紧张的一周后，我们想看部轻松的电影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 lightsome</del></td><td>/ˈlaɪtsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻快的，快活的</td><td>a lightsome step<br>轻快的步子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She crossed the room with a lightsome, dancing step.  她以轻快如舞的步子穿过房间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 slaphappy</del></td><td>/ˈslæpˌhæpi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻率乐观的，晕头转向的</td><td>a slaphappy attitude<br>轻率乐观的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their slaphappy attitude to safety worried the inspector.  他们对安全轻率的态度让检查员担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unconcerned</td><td>/ˌʌnkənˈsɜːrnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不关心的，漠不关心的</td><td>unconcerned about risk<br>对风险漠不关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She seemed entirely unconcerned about the noise from the street.  她似乎完全不在意街上的噪音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 civilized</td><td>/ˈsɪvəˌlaɪzd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>文明的，有教养的</td><td>civilized behavior<br>文明的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They settled the dispute in a calm, civilized manner over tea.  他们喝茶时以平静文明的方式解决了争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 graceful</td><td>/ˈɡreɪsfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>优雅的，优美的</td><td>a graceful dancer<br>姿态优雅的舞者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a graceful turn and landed without a sound.  她优雅地一个转身，落地时毫无声响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 polished</td><td>/ˈpɑːlɪʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的</td><td>polished manners<br>优雅得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His polished manners impressed everyone at the formal dinner.  他优雅得体的举止让正式晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refined</td><td>/rəˈfaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精炼的，纯净的；有教养的</td><td>refined sugar<br>精制糖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refined sugar has been stripped of every impurity.  精制糖已去除了所有杂质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smooth</td><td>/ˈsmuːð/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>温和的，圆滑的；平滑的</td><td>a smooth talker<br>说话圆滑的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a smooth talker who never offends anyone in negotiations.  他谈判时言辞圆滑，从不得罪任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sophisticated</td><td>/səˈfɪstɪkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>复杂精密的；老练的</td><td>a sophisticated system<br>复杂精密的系统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lab uses highly sophisticated equipment to detect the particles.  实验室用极为精密的设备探测这些粒子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 suave</td><td>/ˈswɑːv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>老于世故的，圆滑的</td><td>a suave diplomat<br>圆滑的外交官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The suave diplomat deflected every awkward question gracefully.  这位圆滑的外交官优雅地挡开了每个尴尬问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 svelte</td><td>/svelt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（女人）体态苗条的，优雅的</td><td>a svelte silhouette<br>苗条优雅的身影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her svelte silhouette drew admiring glances across the crowded ballroom.  她苗条优雅的身影在拥挤的舞厅里引来赞赏的目光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 urbane</td><td>/ərˈbeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>彬彬有礼的，文雅的</td><td>an urbane host<br>文雅的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our urbane host guided the conversation with easy wit and grace.  我们文雅的主人以轻松的机智和风度引导着谈话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 careworn</td><td>/ˈkerwɔːrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>憔悴的，饱受忧虑的</td><td>a careworn face<br>憔悴的面容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her careworn face brightened when the children ran into the room.  孩子们跑进房间时，她憔悴的脸上亮了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 boorish</td><td>/ˈbʊrɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁无礼的</td><td>boorish behavior<br>粗鲁无礼的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His boorish behavior at the table embarrassed everyone present.  他在餐桌上粗鲁无礼的举动让在场所有人难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 churlish</td><td>/ˈtʃɜːrlɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁无礼的</td><td>a churlish refusal<br>无礼的拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be churlish to refuse such a generous gift.  拒绝如此丰厚的礼物就太无礼了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loutish</td><td>/ˈlaʊtɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁的</td><td>loutish manners<br>粗鲁的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His loutish manners at the dinner table embarrassed his entire family.  他在餐桌上粗鲁的举止让全家人都很难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncouth</td><td>/ʌnˈkuːθ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁的，无教养的</td><td>uncouth table manners<br>粗野的餐桌举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His uncouth remarks at dinner offended nearly everyone at the table.  他在晚餐上粗鲁的言辞几乎惹恼了同桌所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>decadent</b></td><td>/ˈdekədənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>堕落的，腐败的</td><td>a decadent ruling class<br>腐败堕落的统治阶层</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He described the court as decadent, idle, and blind to reform.  他形容那个宫廷腐败堕落、无所作为、对改革视而不见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debased</td><td>/dəˈbeɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>堕落的，贬损的</td><td>debased taste<br>低劣的品味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He complained about the debased state of public debate.  他抱怨公共辩论的堕落状态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debauched</td><td>/dɪˈbɔtʃt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>放荡的，堕落的</td><td>a debauched lifestyle<br>放荡的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel follows a debauched young lord toward his ruin.  小说讲一位放荡的年轻贵族走向毁灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 degenerate</td><td>/dɪˈdʒenəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、不及物动词</td><td>衰退的，堕落的；恶化</td><td>degenerate into chaos<br>恶化为混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peaceful protest began to degenerate into shouting and shoving.  和平抗议开始恶化为叫骂和推挤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 depraved</td><td>/diˈpreɪvd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>堕落的，道德败坏的</td><td>a depraved appetite<br>堕落的嗜好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the film depraved, which only sold more tickets.  批评者称这部电影堕落，结果票卖得更多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissolute</td><td>/ˈdɪsəluːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>放荡的，无节制的</td><td>a dissolute lifestyle<br>放荡的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel follows a dissolute lifestyle of late nights and squandered fortunes.  小说描写了通宵纵乐、挥霍家财的放荡生活方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perverse</td><td>/pərˈvɜːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反常固执的，任性的</td><td>take perverse pleasure<br>反常地感到快意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He takes perverse pleasure in arguing against every popular opinion.  他反常地乐于反驳每一个流行观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perverted</td><td>/pərˈvɜːrtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>扭曲的，反常堕落的</td><td>a perverted sense of justice<br>扭曲的正义感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He justified the theft with a perverted sense of fairness.  他用一种扭曲的公平感为盗窃辩解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprobate</td><td>/ˈreprəbeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>堕落者，道德败坏的人</td><td>an old reprobate<br>一个老无赖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel's hero is a charming reprobate with no money.  小说的主角是个迷人却身无分文的堕落者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 warped</td><td>/ˈwɔːrpt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（观念）扭曲的，反常的</td><td>a warped sense of humor<br>扭曲的幽默感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel exposes the warped values of that gilded circle.  这部小说揭露了那个纸醉金迷圈子的扭曲价值观。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pure</td><td>/ˈpjʊr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>纯粹的，完全的</td><td>pure coincidence<br>纯属巧合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Meeting her there was pure coincidence, nothing more.  在那里遇见她纯属巧合，别无其他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncorrupted</td><td>/ˌʌnkəˈrʌptɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未腐化的，纯洁的</td><td>uncorrupted by power<br>未被权力腐蚀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained uncorrupted despite decades in machine politics.  尽管在政治机器中浸泡数十年，他仍未被腐蚀。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>decry</b></td><td>/dɪˈkraɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈反对，否定</td><td>decry the new policy<br>强烈反对新政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Local doctors decried the plan to close the only children's clinic.  当地医生强烈反对关闭唯一一家儿童诊所的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belittle</td><td>/bɪˈlɪtəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贬低，轻视</td><td>belittle her achievements<br>贬低她的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't belittle her achievements just because she works quietly.  别因为她默默工作就贬低她的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 denigrate</td><td>/ˈdenɪɡreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诋毁，污蔑</td><td>denigrate a rival<br>诋毁对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He never denigrates his opponents, even after losing badly.  即使惨败，他也从不诋毁对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 denounce</td><td>/dɪˈnaʊns/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开指责</td><td>denounce corruption<br>公开指责腐败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Local leaders publicly denounced the corruption exposed by the newspaper.  当地领导人公开谴责报纸曝光的腐败行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deprecate</td><td>/ˈdeprəkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不赞成，反对；贬低</td><td>deprecate his efforts<br>贬低他的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tends to deprecate his own achievements whenever anyone praises them.  每当有人称赞，他总倾向于贬低自己的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 depreciate</td><td>/dɪˈpriːʃieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贬低…的价值</td><td>depreciate his contribution<br>贬低他的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not depreciate the value of patience in such long negotiations.  不要贬低耐心在这样漫长谈判中的价值。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 derogate</td><td>/ˈderəɡeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>贬损，诋毁</td><td>derogate from an achievement<br>贬损某项成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing should derogate from her remarkable achievement here.  什么都不应贬损她在这里的非凡成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disapprove</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈpruːv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>不赞成，反对</td><td>disapprove of the plan<br>不赞成该计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her parents strongly disapprove of the plan to drop out.  她父母强烈不赞成退学的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dismiss</td><td>/dɪˈsmɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解散，打发走；解雇；不予考虑</td><td>dismiss the class<br>宣布下课</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher dismissed the class ten minutes ahead of time.  老师提前十分钟宣布下课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disparage</td><td>/dɪˈspærɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>贬低；轻蔑地说</td><td>disparage someone's efforts<br>贬低某人的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not disparage her work simply because she is young.  不要仅仅因为她年轻就贬低她的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minimize</td><td>/ˈmɪnɪmaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刻意低估</td><td>minimize the risks<br>刻意淡化风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company tried to minimize the risks in its statement to worried investors.  公司在给焦虑投资者的声明中试图刻意低估风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acclaim</td><td>/əˈkleɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>称赞</td><td>win critical acclaim<br>赢得评论界赞誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won wide critical acclaim across Europe and Asia.  她的第一部小说在欧洲和亚洲赢得了广泛的评论界赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 applaud</td><td>/əˈplɔːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>称赞，赞成；鼓掌</td><td>applaud the decision<br>赞成这一决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teachers applauded the decision to reduce homework for young pupils.  老师们赞成给低年级学生减少作业的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exalt</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːlt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞扬；提升（地位）</td><td>exalt to the skies<br>推崇到极点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics exalt her early novels as masterpieces of the era.  评论家将她早期的小说赞为那个时代的杰作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extol</td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glorify</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːrəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，美化</td><td>glorify war and violence<br>美化战争与暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics said the film glorified war instead of questioning it.  批评者说这部电影美化战争而非质疑它。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 magnify</td><td>/ˈmæɡnəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>放大；夸大</td><td>magnify the problem<br>夸大问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lens magnifies the specimen four hundred times.  这个镜头把样本放大四百倍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>deflect</b></td><td>/dɪˈflekt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使偏斜，使转向；挡开，避开</td><td>deflect the criticism<br>挡开批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The politician deflected every awkward question with a well practiced joke.  那位政客用一个练熟的玩笑挡开每个尴尬的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 divert</td><td>/daɪˈvɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使转向；使消遣</td><td>divert attention from<br>转移对…的注意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The noise diverted my attention from the lecture.  噪音把我的注意力从讲座上引开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 redirect</td><td>/ˌriːdəˈrekt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使改变方向，重新引导</td><td>redirect the funds<br>转拨资金</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city redirected traffic away from the flooded underpass.  该市把车流从被淹的地下通道改道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swing</td><td>/ˈswɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>转向，摆动；秋千</td><td>swing sharply to the left<br>猛地转向左侧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bus swung left to avoid the fallen branch.  公交车向左急转以避开落下的树枝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 veer</td><td>/vɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）转向，（使）改变航线</td><td>veer off course<br>偏离航线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old boat began to veer sharply off course in the wind.  那艘旧船在风中开始急剧偏离航线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wheel</td><td>/ˈwiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>突然转向；轮子</td><td>wheel around suddenly<br>突然转身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wheeled around when he heard footsteps behind him.  他听到身后的脚步声便猛地转身。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 whip</td><td>/ˈwɪp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>猛地转向，急动</td><td>whip around suddenly<br>猛地转过身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She whipped around when she heard footsteps behind her.  听到身后的脚步声，她猛地转过身。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>defray</b></td><td>/dɪˈfreɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支付；偿付；负担费用</td><td>defray travel expenses<br>支付旅行费用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The grant helped defray her travel expenses to the conference.  这笔资助帮她支付了参加会议的旅费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disburse</td><td>/dɪsˈbɜːrs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支付，支出</td><td>disburse the relief funds<br>支付救助款项</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The agency disbursed the grant money in three installments.  该机构分三期支付了这笔拨款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 expend</td><td>/ɪkˈspend/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>花费，耗尽</td><td>expend great effort<br>耗费巨大努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They expended most of their savings on repairing the leaking roof.  他们把大部分积蓄花在修补漏雨的屋顶上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pay</td><td>/ˈpeɪ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>支付，付款</td><td>pay the bill in cash<br>用现金付账</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He paid the whole bill before anyone else noticed.  他在别人注意到之前付掉了整张账单。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acquire</td><td>/əˈkwaɪər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>获得；取得；逐渐养成</td><td>acquire a taste for<br>逐渐喜欢上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took years for him to acquire a taste for olives.  他花了好几年才慢慢喜欢上橄榄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 earn</td><td>/ˈɜːrn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赚得，挣得</td><td>earn a decent living<br>挣得体面的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She earns more from her writing than from teaching.  她写作挣的钱比教书多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gain</td><td>/ˈɡeɪn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>获得，赢得</td><td>gain recognition<br>获得认可</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her essays gained recognition well beyond the campus.  她的文章赢得了远超校园的认可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 garner</td><td>/ˈɡɑːrnər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>获得，积累</td><td>garner support<br>获得支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal garnered support from both major parties.  该提案获得了两大党的支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 procure</td><td>/prəˈkjʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>获得，取得</td><td>procure weapons illegally<br>非法获取武器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smugglers tried to procure weapons through a network of middlemen.  走私者试图通过中间人网络获取武器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 realize</td><td>/ˈriːəˌlaɪz/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>获得（利润）；实现；意识到</td><td>realize a profit<br>获得利润</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They realized a modest profit on the sale of the land.  他们在出售那块地时获得了不多的利润。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 secure</td><td>/sɪˈkjʊr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>牢固的，安全的</td><td>a secure connection<br>安全的连接</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Make sure the ladder is secure before you start climbing.  开始攀爬前确保梯子牢固。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>deign</b></td><td>/deɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（不情愿地）屈尊，俯就</td><td>deign to answer him<br>屈尊回答他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager never deigned to explain his decisions to the staff.  这位经理从不屈尊向员工解释他的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 condescend</td><td>/ˌkɑːndɪˈsend/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>居高临下地对待；俯就</td><td>condescend to his students<br>对学生摆架子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecturer condescended to his students in every class.  这位讲师每节课都对学生摆架子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stoop</td><td>/ˈstuːp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>屈尊，堕落到</td><td>stoop to petty revenge<br>堕落到小肚鸡肠的报复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I never thought he would stoop to reading her mail.  我从没想过他会堕落到偷看她的信件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>delegate</b></td><td>/ˈdelɪˌɡeɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>委派，下放（权力、任务）</td><td>delegate authority to others<br>把权力下放给别人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good leader delegates routine tasks and saves time for planning.  好的领导者会把日常事务移交出去，为规划省下时间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agent</td><td>/ˈeɪdʒənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>代理人，代表</td><td>a literary agent<br>文学代理人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her agent negotiated the contract on her behalf.  她的代理人代表她谈成了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assignee</td><td>/ˌæsəˈniː/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>受托人，受让人</td><td>the assignee of the lease<br>租约的受让人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The assignee assumed all rights and duties under the contract.  受让人承担了合同下的全部权利与义务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commissary</td><td>/ˈkɑːməˌseri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>代表，代理人；军需官</td><td>the papal commissary<br>教皇的代表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bishop sent a commissary to settle the local dispute.  主教派了一名代表去解决当地纠纷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deputy</td><td>/ˈdepjəti/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>代理人，副手</td><td>act as his deputy<br>充当他的副手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor sent his deputy to the opening ceremony.  市长派副手出席了开幕式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emissary</td><td>/ˈeməˌseri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>使者，特使</td><td>a secret emissary<br>秘密特使</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king dispatched an emissary to negotiate the truce.  国王派出一名使者去谈判停战。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 envoy</td><td>/ˈenvɔɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>使节，外交代表</td><td>a special peace envoy<br>和平特使</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The envoy arrived in the capital late on Friday night.  这位使节周五深夜抵达首都。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 legate</td><td>/ˈleɡət/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>使节，教皇特使</td><td>the papal legate<br>教皇特使</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A legate from Rome presided over the long trial.  一位来自罗马的特使主持了这场漫长的审判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minister</td><td>/ˈmɪnəstər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>公使，外交使节；部长</td><td>the minister to France<br>驻法公使</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He served as minister to Spain for almost six years.  他担任驻西班牙公使近六年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proxy</td><td>/ˈprɑːksi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>代理人，代表权</td><td>vote by proxy<br>委托代理投票</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Shareholders unable to attend may vote by proxy instead.  无法出席的股东可以委托代理投票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 representative</td><td>/ˌreprəˈzentətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>代表，代理人</td><td>a union representative<br>工会代表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Each department sends one representative to the weekly meeting.  每个部门派一名代表参加周会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commit</td><td>/kəˈmɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>交托，把…交付给</td><td>commit it to her care<br>把它交由她照管</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They committed the child to the care of her aunt.  他们把孩子交给她姑妈照顾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confide</td><td>/kənˈfaɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>委托，托付；倾诉</td><td>confide a secret to him<br>向他吐露秘密</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She confided the manuscript to her most trusted editor.  她把手稿托付给最信赖的编辑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 consign</td><td>/kənˈsaɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>转交，托付；把…置于（某境地）</td><td>consign goods to a buyer<br>把货物转交买主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court consigned the orphaned children to the care of their aunt.  法院把这几个孤儿转交给他们的姑妈照管。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deliver</td><td>/dɪˈlɪvər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>移交，交付</td><td>deliver the documents personally<br>亲自交付文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The courier delivered the sealed envelope to her office.  快递员把密封信封送到了她的办公室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entrust</td><td>/ɪnˈtrʌst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>委托，托付</td><td>entrust him with the keys<br>把钥匙托付给他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They entrusted the family business to their youngest son.  他们把家族生意托付给了小儿子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repose</td><td>/rɪˈpoʊz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（劳作后的）休息；平静，宁静</td><td>seek repose<br>寻求休息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the long harvest, the villagers enjoyed a few days of repose.  漫长的收割之后，村民们享受了几天的休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transfer</td><td>/trænsˈfɜːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>转移，调动；转让</td><td>transfer the funds<br>转移资金</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bank will transfer the money to her account tomorrow.  银行明天会把钱转到她的账户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transmit</td><td>/trænzˈmɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>传送，发送；传播</td><td>transmit a signal<br>发送信号</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The station transmits its broadcast to villages across the valley.  该电台把节目发送到山谷各处的村庄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vest</td><td>/ˈvest/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>授予（权力）；背心</td><td>vest authority in<br>将权力授予</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The constitution vests final authority in the highest court.  宪法将最终权力授予最高法院。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 give over</td><td>/ɡɪv ˈoʊvər/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>移交，交出</td><td>give over the keys<br>交出钥匙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave over the entire estate to his eldest daughter.  他把整份产业移交给了长女。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hand over</td><td>/hænd ˈoʊvər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>交出，移交</td><td>hand over the documents<br>移交文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The retiring director handed over her files last Friday.  即将退休的主管上周五移交了她的文件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turn over</td><td>/tɜːrn ˈoʊvər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>移交，交给</td><td>turn over the evidence<br>移交证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bank turned over its records to the investigators.  银行把记录移交给了调查人员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hold</td><td>/ˈhoʊld/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>怀有，心存</td><td>hold a grudge<br>心怀怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He still holds a grudge against his former business partner.  他仍然对昔日的生意伙伴心怀怨恨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 keep</td><td>/ˈkiːp/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>遵守，坚持；保留</td><td>keep a promise<br>守约，履行承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He always keeps his promises, however inconvenient they are.  无论多不方便，他总是守约。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 retain</td><td>/rɪˈteɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>保留，留住</td><td>retain the original title<br>保留原名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum retained the building's original facade during the renovation.  翻修时博物馆保留了建筑的原有正面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>delineate</b></td><td>/dɪˈlɪnieɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>描写，描绘</td><td>delineate the main character<br>描绘主要人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel delineates village life in remarkable and affectionate detail.  这部小说以细致而深情的笔触描绘了乡村生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 depict</td><td>/dɪˈpɪkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>描绘，描写</td><td>depict rural life vividly<br>生动描绘乡村生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mural depicts workers building the first railway.  这幅壁画描绘了工人修建第一条铁路的场景。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 display</td><td>/dɪsˈpleɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>展示，陈列；显露</td><td>display the paintings<br>展出这些画作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum displays its rarest coins in a locked glass case.  博物馆把最珍稀的钱币陈列在上锁的玻璃柜里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 draw</td><td>/ˈdrɔː/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>取得，获得；拉，画</td><td>draw a conclusion<br>得出结论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We drew useful lessons from the failed experiment.  我们从失败的实验中获得了有用的教训。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 image</td><td>/ˈɪmədʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>想象，在心中描绘</td><td>image the scene clearly<br>清晰地想象场景</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Try to image the room exactly as it looked then.  试着想象那房间当时的确切模样。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 limn</del></td><td>/ˈlɪm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>描绘，勾画</td><td>limn a memorable portrait<br>勾画出难忘的肖像</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The essay limns her childhood in a few careful strokes.  这篇散文用寥寥几笔勾画出她的童年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paint</td><td>/ˈpeɪnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>涂料，颜料；绘画</td><td>a coat of paint<br>一层涂料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fresh coat of paint transformed the shabby old door.  一层新涂料让破旧的老门焕然一新。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 picture</td><td>/ˈpɪktʃər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>想象，设想；图画</td><td>picture himself as a hero<br>想象自己是英雄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Try to picture the room as it looked a century ago.  试着想象这房间一百年前的样子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 portray</td><td>/pɔrˈtreɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>描绘，刻画</td><td>portray him as a hero<br>把他刻画成英雄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film portrays the scientist as stubborn but kind.  影片把这位科学家刻画得固执却善良。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 render</td><td>/ˈrendər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>交出，给予；使成为</td><td>render assistance<br>提供援助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The neighbors rendered assistance long before the ambulance arrived.  邻居们在救护车到达前很久就提供了援助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 color</td><td>/ˈkʌlər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>渲染，影响（叙述）；着色</td><td>color the account<br>渲染这段叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His prejudice clearly colored the report he submitted to us.  他的偏见明显影响了他交给我们的报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 distort</td><td>/dɪˈstɔːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扭曲，歪曲</td><td>distort the facts<br>歪曲事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Editing the tape that way would distort the facts of the interview.  那样剪辑录音会歪曲采访的事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 falsify</td><td>/ˈfɔːlsəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>篡改，伪造；证明为假</td><td>falsify the records<br>篡改记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An accountant was accused of falsifying the company's records.  一名会计被指控篡改公司账目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 garble</td><td>/ˈɡɑːrbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>曲解，篡改，混淆（以至使无法理解）</td><td>garble the message<br>曲解这条消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bad connection garbled the message beyond all understanding.  信号不好把这条消息混淆得完全无法理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 misrepresent</td><td>/ˌmɪsˌreprɪˈzent/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误传，篡改</td><td>misrepresent the facts<br>歪曲事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics accused the report of misrepresenting the facts to favor one side.  批评者指责这份报告歪曲事实以偏向一方。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 misstate</del></td><td>/mɪsˈsteɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>错误陈述，误报</td><td>misstate the figures<br>错报数字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company was accused of misstating its annual earnings.  该公司被指错报年度收益。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pervert</td><td>/pərˈvɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>曲解，使颠倒；使堕落</td><td>pervert the course of justice<br>妨碍司法公正</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lawyers accused him of trying to pervert the truth in court.  律师指控他试图在法庭上曲解真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 twist</td><td>/ˈtwɪst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>曲解，扭曲；拧</td><td>twist someone's words<br>曲解某人的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not twist my words to suit your own argument.  不要为了你自己的论点曲解我的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 warp</td><td>/wɔːrp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>曲解</td><td>warp the truth<br>曲解真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A careless English translation had warped the author's original meaning.  一个粗心的英文译本曲解了作者的原意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>demote</b></td><td>/ˌdiːˈmoʊt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>降职，降级</td><td>demote him to clerk<br>把他降为职员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board decided to demote him to clerk after the scandal.  丑闻之后，董事会决定把他降为书记员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 break</td><td>/ˈbreɪk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>损坏，打破；间歇</td><td>break the vase<br>打破花瓶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat broke the vase while chasing a moth indoors.  猫在屋里追蛾子时打破了花瓶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bust</td><td>/ˈbʌst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>降级，降职</td><td>bust him to private<br>把他降为士兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sergeant was busted to corporal after the incident.  事件之后这名军士被降为下士。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 degrade</td><td>/dɪˈɡreɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>贬低，使降级；使退化</td><td>degrade the quality<br>降低质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such insulting jokes degrade everyone who repeats them.  这类侮辱性的笑话贬低了每个转述它们的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disrate</del></td><td>/dɪsˈreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>降级，降低职衔</td><td>disrate a careless sailor<br>降低粗心水兵的职衔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain disrated two sailors for repeated negligence.  舰长因屡次疏忽将两名水兵降级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 downgrade</td><td>/ˈdaʊnɡreɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>降级，降低等级</td><td>downgrade the credit rating<br>下调信用评级</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Analysts downgraded the company after two weak quarterly reports.  两份疲弱季报后分析师下调了该公司评级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reduce</td><td>/rəˈduːs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减少，降低</td><td>reduce the cost sharply<br>大幅降低成本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New machines reduced the factory's energy use by half.  新机器把工厂的能耗降低了一半。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 advance</td><td>/ədˈvæns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>前进；促进；预付款</td><td>advance a theory<br>提出一种理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The army advanced slowly through the thick forest at dawn.  黎明时军队缓慢穿过密林前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 elevate</td><td>/ˈelɪveɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提升，提高；使兴奋</td><td>elevate the mind<br>提升心智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good literature can elevate the mind and refine one's taste.  优秀的文学能提升心智、陶冶品味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 promote</td><td>/prəˈmoʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>促进，助长；晋升</td><td>promote economic growth<br>促进经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The campaign aims to promote healthy eating among children.  该活动旨在促进儿童健康饮食。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 raise</td><td>/ˈreɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提出（问题）；举起，提高</td><td>raise an objection<br>提出反对意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He raised an objection just before the vote was taken.  就在投票前他提出了反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dependable</b></td><td>/dɪˈpendəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可靠的，值得信赖的</td><td>a dependable old car<br>可靠的旧车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We needed a dependable driver who would arrive on time every day.  我们需要一位每天都能准时到达的可靠司机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 calculable</td><td>/ˈkælkjələbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可计算的，可估量的</td><td>calculable risk<br>可估量的风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers prefer a calculable risk to an unknown one.  工程师宁要可估量的风险，也不要未知风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reliable</td><td>/rɪˈlaɪəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可靠的，可信赖的</td><td>a reliable source<br>可靠的消息来源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The story came from a reliable source inside the ministry.  这条消息来自部内一个可靠的来源。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 responsible</td><td>/riˈspɑːnsəbəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可靠的，负责任的</td><td>a responsible employee<br>尽责可靠的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need a responsible person to lock up each night.  我们需要一个可靠的人每晚锁门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 safe</td><td>/ˈseɪf/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>安全的，可靠的</td><td>a safe choice<br>稳妥的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Taking the train is the safe choice in this weather.  这种天气坐火车是稳妥的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 secure</td><td>/sɪˈkjʊr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>牢固的，安全的</td><td>a secure connection<br>安全的连接</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Make sure the ladder is secure before you start climbing.  开始攀爬前确保梯子牢固。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 solid</td><td>/ˈsɑːlɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗壮结实的</td><td>a solid, heavy build<br>粗壮结实的体格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wrestler had a solid, heavy build unlike his slender opponent.  这名摔跤手体格粗壮结实，与瘦削的对手截然不同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trustable</td><td>/ˈtrʌstəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可信赖的</td><td>a trustable witness<br>可信赖的证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only two of the sources proved genuinely trustable.  这些消息来源中只有两个确实可信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trustworthy</td><td>/ˈtrʌstwɜːrði/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得信赖的，可靠的</td><td>a trustworthy friend<br>值得信赖的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He proved a trustworthy friend during her worst year.  在她最糟的一年里他证明是值得信赖的朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undependable</td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈpɛndəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可靠的</td><td>an undependable memory<br>不可靠的记忆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His undependable attendance eventually cost him the job.  他不可靠的出勤最终让他丢了工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unreliable</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈlaɪəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可靠的，靠不住的</td><td>an unreliable narrator<br>不可靠的叙述者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early weather forecasts here are notoriously unreliable.  这里早期的天气预报出名地靠不住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unsafe</td><td>/ʌnˈseɪf/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不安全的，靠不住的</td><td>unsafe drinking water<br>不安全的饮用水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors declared the old bridge unsafe for heavy trucks.  检查人员宣布这座旧桥不宜通行重型卡车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 untrustworthy</td><td>/ʌnˈtrʌstwɜːrði/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不值得信任的</td><td>an untrustworthy partner<br>不可信的合作伙伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two witnesses described him as lazy and untrustworthy.  两名证人形容他懒惰且不可信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>deplorable</b></td><td>/dɪˈplɔːrəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>糟糕的，恶劣的；可鄙的</td><td>deplorable working conditions<br>恶劣的工作条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors found deplorable working conditions inside the crowded garment factory.  检查人员在拥挤的服装厂内发现了恶劣的工作条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 despicable</td><td>/dɪˈspɪkəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人鄙视的</td><td>a despicable act<br>令人鄙视的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stealing from a charity is a despicable way to make money.  从慈善机构偷钱是一种令人鄙视的赚钱方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dirty</td><td>/ˈdɜːrti/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>肮脏的；卑鄙的</td><td>dirty windows<br>脏污的窗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children came home with dirty hands and happy faces.  孩子们回家时手很脏，脸上却很开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grubby</td><td>/ˈɡrʌbi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>肮脏的；卑劣的</td><td>grubby hands<br>脏兮兮的手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boy came home with grubby knees and a torn shirt.  男孩回家时膝盖脏兮兮，衬衫也破了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lousy</td><td>/ˈlaʊzi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多得很的，充满的；糟糕的</td><td>lousy with tourists<br>游客多得很</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In August the town is lousy with tourists from every corner of Europe.  八月这座小镇挤满了来自欧洲各地的游客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mean</td><td>/miːn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑贱的；吝啬的</td><td>a mean little room<br>简陋卑贱的小屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family lived in a mean little room above a noisy printing shop.  一家人住在嘈杂印刷店楼上一间简陋的小屋里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nasty</td><td>/ˈnæsti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，肮脏的；恶劣的</td><td>a nasty smell<br>难闻的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A nasty stain spread slowly across the pale carpet.  一块难看的污渍在浅色地毯上慢慢蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paltry</td><td>/ˈpɔːltri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无价值的；微不足道的</td><td>a paltry sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered a paltry sum for a painting worth thousands of dollars.  他们为一幅价值数千美元的画只出了微不足道的一笔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scurvy</td><td>/ˈskɜːrvi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>下流的，让人鄙视的</td><td>a scurvy trick<br>下流的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheating an old friend was a scurvy trick nobody could excuse.  欺骗老友是种下流手段，谁也无法原谅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wretched</td><td>/ˈretʃɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极差的；沮丧的</td><td>wretched living conditions<br>恶劣的居住条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Families lived in wretched conditions beside the flooded riverbank.  一些家庭在泛滥的河岸边过着极差的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 admirable</td><td>/ˈædmərəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人钦佩的；极好的</td><td>admirable courage<br>令人钦佩的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her patience with difficult students was truly admirable.  她对难教学生的耐心确实令人钦佩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commendable</td><td>/kəˈmendəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得赞扬的</td><td>commendable honesty<br>值得赞扬的诚实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rescue team showed commendable calm during the long night.  救援队在漫长的夜里表现出值得赞扬的镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 creditable</td><td>/ˈkredətəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得称赞的；相当好的</td><td>a creditable performance<br>值得称赞的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young violinist gave a creditable account of the concerto.  年轻小提琴手对协奏曲的演绎值得称赞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laudable</td><td>/ˈlɔːdəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得赞扬的</td><td>a laudable effort<br>值得赞扬的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her attempt to save the library was laudable though it failed.  她拯救图书馆的尝试虽失败却值得赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meritorious</td><td>/ˌmerəˈtɔːriəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有功的，值得称赞的</td><td>meritorious service<br>有功的服务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He received a medal for meritorious conduct under heavy fire.  他因在猛烈炮火下的有功表现获得奖章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praiseworthy</td><td>/ˈpreɪzˌwɜːrði/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得赞扬的</td><td>a praiseworthy effort<br>值得赞扬的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their decision to return the money was entirely praiseworthy.  他们归还钱款的决定完全值得赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dichotomy</b></td><td>/daɪˈkɑːtəmi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>对立</td><td>a false dichotomy<br>虚假的二分对立</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The book rejects the simple dichotomy between nature and human culture.  这本书否认自然与人类文化之间的简单对立。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bifurcation</td><td>/ˌbaɪfərˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分岔，一分为二</td><td>the bifurcation of the river<br>河流的分岔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sharp bifurcation of the path confused the hikers.  小径突然分岔，把徒步者弄糊涂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 breakup</td><td>/ˈbreɪˌkʌp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>解体，分裂</td><td>the breakup of the empire<br>帝国的解体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Historians still argue about the breakup of the old empire.  史学家仍在争论那个旧帝国的解体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cleavage</td><td>/ˈkliːvədʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>分裂，裂开</td><td>a deep political cleavage<br>深刻的政治分裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A deep cleavage split the party into two hostile wings.  一道深刻的裂痕把该党分成两个敌对派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 division</td><td>/dɪˈvɪʒən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分歧，不和；分开，部门</td><td>deep division over policy<br>政策上的深刻分歧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is deep division within the committee about the budget.  委员会内部在预算上存在深刻分歧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 partition</td><td>/pɑːrˈtɪʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>分割，划分</td><td>the partition of the estate<br>地产的分割</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brothers argued for years over the partition of the family land.  兄弟们为家族土地的分割争执了多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 schism</td><td>/ˈskɪzəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分裂，派系对立</td><td>a bitter schism<br>严重的分裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bitter schism split the party into two hostile factions.  一场激烈的分裂把该党撕成两个敌对派系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 scission</del></td><td>/ˈsɪʒən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>切断，分离，分裂</td><td>a clean scission<br>干净的切断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty produced a clean scission between the two neighboring provinces.  那份条约使两个相邻省份彻底分离。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 split</td><td>/ˈsplɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>分裂，分开</td><td>split into groups<br>分成小组</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee split into two camps over the proposal.  委员会因该提案分裂成两派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sundering</td><td>/ˈsʌndərɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分离，割裂</td><td>the sundering of old ties<br>旧关系的割裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The war brought a painful sundering of old family ties.  战争带来了旧日家族关系的痛苦割裂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unification</td><td>/ˌjuːnəfəˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>统一，联合</td><td>the unification of the country<br>国家的统一</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty prepared the way for the unification of the states.  这项条约为各邦统一铺平了道路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 union</td><td>/ˈjuːnjən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>联合，结合；工会</td><td>a lasting union of states<br>各州的持久联合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two colonies formed a lasting union after long talks.  两块殖民地经长期谈判形成持久联合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dictate</b></td><td>/ˈdɪkteɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（仗着地位、权力）下令</td><td>dictate terms to them<br>向他们强行规定条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The victors dictated harsh terms to the defeated coastal cities.  战胜者向战败的沿海城市强行规定苛刻条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 call</td><td>/ˈkɔːl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>召集，召开</td><td>call a special meeting<br>召开特别会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chairman called an emergency meeting for Monday morning.  主席召集了周一上午的紧急会议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decree</td><td>/dɪˈkriː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>法令，命令</td><td>issue a decree<br>颁布一项法令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government issued a decree banning imports of that timber.  政府颁布法令禁止进口那种木材。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 direct</td><td>/dəˈrekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>直接的，径直的</td><td>a direct flight<br>直飞航班</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ask for a direct answer instead of a long explanation.  要个直接的答复，而不是长篇解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impose</td><td>/ɪmˈpoʊz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>强加，征收</td><td>impose a heavy tax<br>征收重税</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court imposed a strict curfew on the district.  法院对该区强加了严格宵禁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mandate</td><td>/ˈmænˌdeɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>强制要求，命令</td><td>mandate annual safety checks<br>强制要求年度安检</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new law mandates helmets for all young riders.  新法规定所有年轻骑手必须戴头盔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ordain</td><td>/ɔːrˈdeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>命令，规定；任命神职</td><td>ordain that all must attend<br>规定所有人必须出席</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old charter ordained that meetings be held every spring.  旧宪章规定会议须于每年春季举行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 order</td><td>/ˈɔːrdər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>秩序，条理；命令</td><td>restore public order<br>恢复公共秩序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police worked all night to restore order in the city.  警察通宵工作以恢复城市的秩序。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prescribe</td><td>/prɪˈskraɪb/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>规定，指令；开药</td><td>prescribe strict procedures<br>规定严格的程序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rules prescribe exactly how ballots must be counted.  规则明确规定了选票必须如何清点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cancel</td><td>/ˈkænsəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>取消，撤销；抵消</td><td>cancel the meeting<br>取消会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They canceled the outdoor concert because of the heavy rain.  因为大雨，他们取消了露天音乐会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 countermand</td><td>/ˌkaʊntərˈmænd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>取消，撤销</td><td>countermand an order<br>撤销一道命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general countermanded the order before the troops reached the river.  将军在部队抵达河边之前撤销了那道命令。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rescind</td><td>/rɪˈsɪnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>废除，取消</td><td>rescind an offer<br>撤回报价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company rescinded the job offer without explanation.  公司未加解释便撤回了聘用通知。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>discreet</b></td><td>/dɪˈskriːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言行）谨慎的</td><td>a discreet inquiry<br>谨慎的询问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A discreet inquiry into his background turned up nothing worth reporting.  对他背景的谨慎查访没查出任何值得上报的事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intelligent</td><td>/ɪnˈtelədʒənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，聪明的</td><td>an intelligent decision<br>明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Selling the old plant was an intelligent decision.  卖掉旧厂是个明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 judicious</td><td>/dʒuˈdɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，慎重的</td><td>a judicious choice<br>明智的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A judicious use of savings carried the family through two difficult years.  明智地使用积蓄让这家人度过了两个艰难的年头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prudent</td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imprudent</td><td>/ɪmˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的</td><td>an imprudent investment<br>不明智的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Borrowing so much money at that rate was clearly imprudent.  以那样的利率借这么多钱显然是不明智的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indiscreet</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈskriːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不慎重的，轻率的</td><td>an indiscreet comment<br>轻率的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One indiscreet email to a colleague cost him the promotion.  一封写给同事的轻率邮件让他失去了升职机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 injudicious</td><td>/ˌɪndʒuˈdɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，欠考虑的</td><td>an injudicious remark<br>欠考虑的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One injudicious comment cost him the whole negotiation.  一句欠考虑的话让他输掉了整场谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
