<tr><td><del><b>disenchant</b></del></td><td>/ˌdɪsɪnˈtʃænt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使清醒</td><td>disenchant the young voters<br>使年轻选民清醒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years in politics disenchanted her with grand promises.  多年从政使她对宏大承诺不再抱幻想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disabuse</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈbjuːz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打消错误念头，纠正</td><td>disabuse him of the notion<br>打消他的错误念头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took hours to disabuse him of the notion that the test was simple.  花了好几个小时才打消他认为考试很简单的错误念头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 undeceive</del></td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈsiːv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使醒悟，使不再受骗</td><td>undeceive the public<br>使公众醒悟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone must undeceive her before she invests everything in that scheme.  必须有人让她醒悟，别把全部钱投进那个计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 beguile</td><td>/bɪˈɡaɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗；使着迷</td><td>beguile the audience<br>令观众着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician could beguile an audience for a full hour.  这位魔术师能让观众着迷整整一小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cozen</td><td>/ˈkʌzn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>诱骗</td><td>cozen him into agreeing<br>诱骗他同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler cozened elderly widows out of their modest life savings.  那个骗子诱骗年老的寡妇们交出微薄的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 delude</td><td>/dɪˈluːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，使误信</td><td>delude oneself<br>自欺欺人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not delude yourself into thinking the danger has passed.  别欺骗自己，以为危险已经过去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dupe</td><td>/duːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>dupe trusting investors<br>欺骗轻信的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scheme duped trusting investors out of nearly a million dollars.  这个骗局欺骗轻信的投资者，骗走近百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fool</td><td>/ˈfuːl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>傻瓜，蠢人；愚弄</td><td>make a fool of oneself<br>让自己出丑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt like a fool after believing the obvious lie.  信了那个明显的谎言后，他觉得自己像个傻瓜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gull</td><td>/ɡʌl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>gull the unwary<br>欺骗粗心的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler gulled dozens of elderly investors out of their savings.  骗子欺骗了数十位老年投资者，骗走他们的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hoax</td><td>/hoʊks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>an elaborate hoax<br>精心设计的骗局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph turned out to be an elaborate hoax created by students.  那张照片原来是学生们精心设计的骗局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hoodwink</td><td>/ˈhʊdwɪŋk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>hoodwink the voters<br>欺骗选民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company hoodwinked customers with promises it could never possibly keep.  这家公司用根本无法兑现的承诺欺骗顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 misguide</td><td>/mɪsˈɡaɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误导，使误入歧途</td><td>misguide the public<br>误导公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The advertisement was designed to misguide careless buyers.  这则广告旨在误导粗心的买家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 misinform</td><td>/ˌmɪsɪnˈfɔːrm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误告，提供错误信息</td><td>misinform the public<br>误导公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone misinformed us about the museum's closing time last Saturday.  上周六有人把博物馆的关门时间告错了我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mislead</td><td>/mɪsˈliːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误导，使误信</td><td>mislead investors<br>误导投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chart was designed to mislead careless readers.  这张图表意在误导不细心的读者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dishearten</b></td><td>/dɪsˈhɑːrtn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧，使失去信心</td><td>disheartened by the news<br>因这消息而沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The string of early losses disheartened even the most loyal fans.  开局连输让最忠实的球迷也失去了信心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chill</td><td>/ˈtʃɪl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使胆寒，使沮丧；寒冷</td><td>chill his enthusiasm<br>打消他的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The news of the layoffs chilled everyone in the office.  裁员的消息让办公室里的每个人都心生寒意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 daunt</td><td>/dɔːnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使胆怯，吓倒</td><td>daunt the bravest soldiers<br>吓倒最勇敢的士兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The steep climb ahead did not daunt the experienced mountain guide.  前方陡峭的攀登丝毫没有吓倒这位经验丰富的山地导游。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demoralize</td><td>/dɪˈmɔːrəlaɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使士气低落；贬低，使堕落</td><td>demoralize the troops<br>使部队士气低落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repeated pay cuts demoralized the staff more than the long hours.  反复减薪比长时间工作更让员工士气低落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discourage</td><td>/dɪsˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧，使气馁；劝阻</td><td>discourage him from trying<br>劝他不要尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early failure should not discourage a young researcher.  早期的失败不该让年轻研究者气馁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dismay</td><td>/dɪsˈmeɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使失去勇气；使不安，使焦虑</td><td>dismay his supporters<br>使支持者沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The abrupt policy reversal dismayed his supporters and delighted his rivals.  政策的突然反转让他的支持者沮丧，让对手高兴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 dispirit</del></td><td>/dɪˈspɪrɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧，使气馁</td><td>dispirit the whole team<br>使全队士气低落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The endless rain dispirited even the most cheerful campers.  连绵的阴雨让最开朗的营员也沮丧起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frustrate</td><td>/ˈfrʌstreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻挠，使受挫；使沮丧</td><td>frustrate one's plans<br>使计划受挫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow frustrated our plans to drive home.  大雪打乱了我们开车回家的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unnerve</td><td>/ʌnˈnɜːrv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使紧张不安，使气馁</td><td>unnerve the young witness<br>使年轻证人紧张不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden silence in the courtroom unnerved the young witness completely.  法庭上突然的寂静让年轻证人完全紧张起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 embolden</td><td>/ɪmˈboʊldən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，使大胆</td><td>embolden the rebels<br>壮了叛乱者的胆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early success emboldened her to ask for a promotion.  初期的成功让她有胆量要求升职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 encourage</td><td>/ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，激励；促进</td><td>encourage students to read<br>鼓励学生阅读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers encourage students to ask difficult questions.  好老师鼓励学生提出有难度的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hearten</td><td>/ˈhɑːrtn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>给予鼓励，鼓舞</td><td>hearten the troops<br>鼓舞部队士气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd's cheering heartened the runners on the last steep hill.  观众的欢呼在最后一个陡坡上鼓舞了跑者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nerve</td><td>/nɜːrv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>（坚强的）意志，勇气</td><td>lose one's nerve<br>失去勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lost his nerve at the last moment and never asked the question.  他在最后一刻失去了勇气，始终没有问出那个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 steel</td><td>/ˈstiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使坚强，使下决心</td><td>steel oneself for bad news<br>为坏消息鼓起勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She steeled herself and walked into the interview room.  她鼓起勇气走进面试室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>disjunctive</b></td><td>/dɪsˈdʒʌŋktɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>分离的</td><td>a disjunctive structure<br>分离的结构</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two clauses form a disjunctive structure that offers readers a clear choice.  这两个分句构成分离的结构，给读者一个明确的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conjunctive</td><td>/kənˈdʒʌŋktɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>连接的，联合的</td><td>a conjunctive adverb<br>连接副词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two clauses are joined by a conjunctive phrase.  这两个分句由一个连接短语相连。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 connective</td><td>/kəˈnektɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>连接的，联结的</td><td>connective tissue<br>结缔组织</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cartilage is a tough connective tissue found in joints.  软骨是关节中一种坚韧的结缔组织。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>disprove</b></td><td>/ˌdɪsˈpruːv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>反驳，证明为假</td><td>disprove the theory<br>驳倒这一理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Magellan's voyage finally disproved the belief in a flat earth.  麦哲伦的航行最终驳倒了地球是平的这一信念。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belie</td><td>/bɪˈlaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>与…相对立；与…相矛盾</td><td>belie his claims<br>与他的说法矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company's own records belie his claims about the accident.  公司自己的记录与他关于事故的说法相矛盾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confound</td><td>/kənˈfaʊnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑；证明为假，证伪</td><td>confound the critics<br>让批评者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The late victory confounded the critics who had predicted failure.  最后的胜利让预言失败的批评者困惑不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 confute</del></td><td>/kənˈfjuːt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>驳倒，证明有误</td><td>confute the old theory<br>驳倒旧理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single careful experiment confuted decades of speculation.  一次严谨的实验驳倒了数十年的猜测。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debunk</td><td>/ˌdiːˈbʌŋk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>揭穿…的真面目</td><td>debunk the myth<br>揭穿这个神话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single careful experiment debunked the theory that had misled everyone.  一次严谨的实验揭穿了那个误导所有人的理论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disconfirm</del></td><td>/ˌdɪskənˈfɜrm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证明为假，否证</td><td>disconfirm the hypothesis<br>否证该假设</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New data disconfirmed the theory proposed a decade earlier.  新数据否证了十年前提出的理论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discredit</td><td>/dɪsˈkredɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使丧失名誉；怀疑</td><td>discredit a witness<br>质疑证人可信度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer tried to discredit the witness with old records.  律师试图用旧档案质疑证人的可信度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 falsify</td><td>/ˈfɔːlsəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>篡改，伪造；证明为假</td><td>falsify the records<br>篡改记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An accountant was accused of falsifying the company's records.  一名会计被指控篡改公司账目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rebut</td><td>/rɪˈbʌt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>反驳，驳斥</td><td>rebut the accusation<br>反驳这项指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She rebutted every charge with documents and dated photographs.  她用文件和标注日期的照片反驳了每项指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refute</td><td>/rɪˈfjuːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>驳倒，反驳</td><td>refute the claim<br>驳斥这一说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scientist used new data to refute the claim about rising temperatures.  这位科学家用新数据反驳了关于气温上升的说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shoot down</td><td>/ʃuːt ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>驳倒，否决；击落</td><td>shoot down the plan<br>否决该计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee shot down his budget request within ten minutes.  委员会十分钟内就否决了他的预算申请。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 confirm</td><td>/kənˈfɜːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，确认，进一步确定</td><td>confirm the findings<br>证实这些发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Later tests confirmed what the first team had suspected.  后来的检测证实了第一个团队的猜测。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 establish</td><td>/ɪˈstæblɪʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>确证，证实；建立</td><td>establish the facts beyond doubt<br>确证事实无疑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The investigation established the facts beyond any reasonable doubt.  调查毫无疑问地确证了事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prove</td><td>/ˈpruːv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>证明，证实</td><td>prove his innocence<br>证明他无罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New evidence finally proved his innocence after eleven long years.  新证据在漫长的十一年后终于证明他无罪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 validate</td><td>/ˈvælədeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，确认；使有效</td><td>validate the results<br>验证这些结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Independent labs validated the results within three months.  独立实验室在三个月内验证了这些结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 verify</td><td>/ˈverɪfaɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>证实，核实</td><td>verify the data<br>核实这些数据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two independent labs will verify the results before publication.  两家独立实验室将在发表前核实这些结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>distinctive</b></td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋktɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>特征性的，典型的</td><td>a distinctive style<br>独特的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her distinctive style makes each painting recognizable from across the gallery.  她独特的风格使每幅画在展厅另一端就能被认出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disparate</td><td>/ˈdɪspərət/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迥然不同的，全然不同的</td><td>disparate viewpoints<br>迥然不同的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee somehow reconciled disparate viewpoints into a single workable proposal.  委员会竟把迥然不同的观点整合成一份可行的提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissimilar</td><td>/dɪˈsɪmələr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不同的，不相似的</td><td>wholly dissimilar styles<br>完全不同的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two novels treat love in dissimilar ways.  这两部小说以不同的方式处理爱情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distant</td><td>/ˈdɪstənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷淡的，疏远的；遥远的</td><td>a distant manner<br>冷淡的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was polite but distant throughout the whole dinner.  整个晚餐期间她客气却冷淡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinct</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>清晰的，明显的</td><td>a distinct impression<br>清晰的印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a distinct difference between the two dialects.  这两种方言之间有明显差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinguishable</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可区分的，可辨别的</td><td>clearly distinguishable from others<br>与其他明显可区分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The twins are barely distinguishable except for a small scar.  这对双胞胎除了一道小疤几乎无法区分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diverse</td><td>/daɪˈvɜːrs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不同的，多样的</td><td>diverse backgrounds<br>各不相同的背景</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The class brings together students from diverse cultural backgrounds.  这个班汇集了来自不同文化背景的学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unalike</del></td><td>/ˌʌnəˈlaɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相像的，不同的</td><td>utterly unalike in temper<br>性情上完全不像</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brothers looked similar but were entirely unalike in temper.  兄弟俩外貌相似，但性情完全不同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unlike</td><td>/ˌʌnˈlaɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、介词</td><td>不同的；不像</td><td>unlike his brother<br>与他兄弟不同</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unlike his brother, he never enjoyed noisy crowds.  与他兄弟不同，他从不喜欢喧闹的人群。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 characteristic</td><td>/ˌkerəktəˈrɪstɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>典型的，独特的</td><td>with characteristic modesty<br>以他惯有的谦逊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered with characteristic patience and a faint smile.  他以惯有的耐心和淡淡的微笑作答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 classic</td><td>/ˈklæsɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>典型的，经典的</td><td>a classic case of denial<br>典型的否认案例</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These symptoms are classic signs of severe dehydration.  这些症状是严重脱水的典型表现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diagnostic</td><td>/ˌdaɪəɡˈnɑːstɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>特征性的；诊断的</td><td>a diagnostic feature<br>特征性的特点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The narrow leaves are a diagnostic feature of this species.  狭长的叶子是该物种的特征性特点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discriminating</td><td>/dɪsˈkrɪməˌneɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有差别对待的；有鉴别力的</td><td>policies discriminating against women<br>歧视女性的政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court struck down policies discriminating against women in hiring.  法院废除了在招聘中歧视女性的政策。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinguishing</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有区别性的，特征性的</td><td>a distinguishing mark<br>识别标记</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bird's red crest is its main distinguishing mark.  这种鸟的红冠是它主要的识别标记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 identifying</td><td>/aɪˈdentəˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>用于辨认的，标识性的</td><td>identifying details<br>身份识别信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report removed all identifying details of the patients.  报告删去了病人所有身份识别信息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peculiar</td><td>/pəˈkjuːliər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奇怪的；特有的</td><td>a peculiar smell<br>一股怪味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a peculiar smell coming from the basement.  地下室传来一股奇怪的味道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 symptomatic</td><td>/ˌsɪmptəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有症状的；典型表征的</td><td>symptomatic of deeper trouble<br>更深问题的表征</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delays are symptomatic of a badly managed project.  这些延误是项目管理混乱的典型表现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 typical</td><td>/ˈtɪpəkəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>典型的，一贯的</td><td>a typical example<br>典型的例子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This building is a typical example of the local style.  这栋建筑是当地风格的典型代表。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 identical</td><td>/aɪˈdentɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>完全相同的，同一的</td><td>virtually identical results<br>几乎完全相同的结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two samples produced identical readings on every machine.  两个样本在每台机器上都得出相同读数。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indistinguishable</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法区分的，难以辨别的</td><td>indistinguishable from the original<br>与原件无法区分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The copy was almost indistinguishable from the original painting.  这份复制品与原画几乎无法区分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 same</td><td>/ˈseɪm/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相同的，同样的</td><td>exactly the same size<br>完全相同的尺寸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both machines run at the same speed under normal load.  两台机器在正常负载下以相同速度运行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 atypical</td><td>/ˌeɪˈtɪpɪkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非典型的，不寻常的</td><td>an atypical case<br>一个非典型病例</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This is an atypical response to a very common medicine.  这是对一种常用药物的非典型反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncharacteristic</td><td>/ˌʌnkerəktəˈrɪstɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不典型的，不像其一贯作风的</td><td>uncharacteristic silence from him<br>他罕见的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sharp reply was quite uncharacteristic of her.  她那句尖刻的回答很不像她平日的样子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 untypical</td><td>/ʌnˈtɪpɪkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非典型的，不常见的</td><td>an untypical winter<br>不典型的冬天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This mild, dry winter is untypical for the region.  这个温和干燥的冬天对该地区来说很不典型。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>doctrinaire</b></td><td>/ˌdɑːktrəˈner/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>教条主义的，照本宣科的</td><td>a doctrinaire approach<br>教条主义的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His doctrinaire approach left no room for evidence that contradicted the theory.  他教条主义的做法容不下任何与理论相悖的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dogmatic</td><td>/dɔːɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>武断的，自以为是的</td><td>dogmatic assertions<br>武断的断言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is too dogmatic to consider any alternative explanation.  他太武断，不肯考虑任何别的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 latitudinarian</del></td><td>/ˌlætɪtudɪˈneriən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>宽容的，不拘教条的</td><td>a latitudinarian attitude<br>宽容不拘的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His latitudinarian views angered the stricter members of the church.  他宽容不拘的观点激怒了教会中较严格的成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>domineer</b></del></td><td>/ˌdɑːməˈnɪr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>专横跋扈，作威作福</td><td>domineer over his staff<br>专横地对待下属</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He liked to domineer over his staff instead of listening to their advice.  他喜欢专横地对待下属，而不是听取他们的建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tyrannize</td><td>/ˈtɪrənaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>专横统治，欺压</td><td>tyrannize over his family<br>在家中作威作福</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The foreman tyrannized the younger workers for years.  这个工头多年来一直欺压年轻工人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>drab</b></td><td>/dræb/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，无聊的</td><td>drab gray walls<br>单调的灰墙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They painted over the drab gray walls with something warmer and brighter.  他们把单调的灰墙涂上了更暖更亮的颜色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arid</td><td>/ˈærɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>枯燥无趣的；干燥的</td><td>an arid lecture<br>枯燥的讲座</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor's arid lecture emptied half the seats before the break.  这位教授枯燥乏味的讲座在课间前就空了一半座位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreary</td><td>/ˈdrɪri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调乏味的</td><td>a dreary afternoon<br>沉闷的下午</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a dreary afternoon indoors watching rain streak the windows.  我们在室内度过一个沉闷的下午，看着雨水划过窗玻璃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 drudging</del></td><td>/ˈdrʌdʒɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>苦干的，单调乏味的</td><td>drudging office work<br>枯燥的办公室工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of drudging clerical work dulled his early ambition.  多年枯燥的文书苦工磨钝了他早年的雄心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 humdrum</td><td>/ˈhʌmdrʌm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调乏味的；平淡的</td><td>a humdrum routine<br>单调乏味的日常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left the humdrum job to travel and write full time.  她辞掉那份单调乏味的工作，去全职旅行和写作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 Jading</del></td><td>/ˈdʒeɪdɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌倦的，使人生厌</td><td>a jading routine<br>令人厌倦的日常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of jading paperwork dulled his early enthusiasm.  多年令人厌倦的文书工作磨掉了他早年的热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jejune</td><td>/dʒɪˈdʒuːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣乏味的</td><td>a jejune argument<br>乏味浅薄的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics dismissed the sequel as jejune and repetitive.  评论家认为这部续集乏味且重复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monochromatic</td><td>/ˌmɑːnəkroʊˈmætɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单色的</td><td>a monochromatic palette<br>单色的配色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The designer chose a monochromatic scheme of soft grays.  设计师选了柔灰的单色方案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monotonous</td><td>/məˈnɑːtənəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，无变化的</td><td>a monotonous voice<br>单调的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His monotonous voice made the afternoon lecture almost impossible to sit through.  他单调的声音让下午的讲座几乎难以听完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pedestrian</td><td>/pəˈdestriən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平庸无奇的，令人厌倦的</td><td>a pedestrian style<br>平庸的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reviewer called the plot pedestrian and the dialogue painfully flat.  评论者称其情节平庸，对话乏味得令人难受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ponderous</td><td>/ˈpɑːndərəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉重的</td><td>ponderous machinery<br>笨重的机器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ponderous machinery filled the workshop and shook the floor all day.  笨重的机器占满车间，整天震动地板。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stale</td><td>/steɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧乏味的</td><td>stale jokes<br>老掉牙的笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His stale jokes made the audience shift restlessly in their seats.  他老掉牙的笑话让观众在座位上不安地挪动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stodgy</td><td>/ˈstɑːdʒi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古板守旧的，乏味的</td><td>a stodgy old textbook<br>一本乏味的旧教材</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The course relied on a stodgy old textbook nobody enjoyed reading.  这门课用的是一本没人爱读的乏味旧教材。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tedious</td><td>/ˈtiːdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长乏味的</td><td>a tedious explanation<br>冗长乏味的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager gave a tedious explanation of the new filing system.  经理对新的归档系统作了冗长乏味的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tiresome</td><td>/ˈtaɪərsəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌烦的</td><td>a tiresome chore<br>烦人的杂事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Washing dishes by hand is a tiresome nightly chore.  手洗碗碟是每晚烦人的活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wearisome</td><td>/ˈwɪrisəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌倦的，乏味的</td><td>a wearisome journey<br>令人疲乏的旅程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wearisome journey took three days by slow train.  这段疲乏的旅程坐慢车要三天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 absorbing</td><td>/əbˈzɔːrbɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，吸引人的</td><td>an absorbing novel<br>引人入胜的小说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found her account of the voyage utterly absorbing.  我觉得她对航行的叙述极其引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engaging</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>an engaging personality<br>讨人喜欢的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His engaging manner made strangers feel comfortable within minutes.  他讨人喜欢的举止让陌生人几分钟内就放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engrossing</td><td>/ɪnˈɡroʊsɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，令人专注的</td><td>an engrossing story<br>引人入胜的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary was so engrossing that nobody left early.  这部纪录片如此引人入胜，没人提早离场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gripping</td><td>/ˈɡrɪpɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>扣人心弦的，紧张的</td><td>a gripping thriller<br>扣人心弦的惊悚片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final chapter is gripping from start to finish.  最后一章从头到尾都扣人心弦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 interesting</td><td>/ˈɪntrəstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有趣的，引人入胜的</td><td>an interesting proposal<br>一个有趣的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised an interesting objection nobody had anticipated.  她提出了一个无人预料到的有趣反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intriguing</td><td>/ˌɪnˈtriːɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人好奇的，有趣的</td><td>an intriguing question<br>引人好奇的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised an intriguing point that nobody had considered.  她提出了一个无人考虑过的有趣观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 involving</td><td>/ɪnˈvɑːlvɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，扣人心弦的</td><td>an involving story<br>引人入胜的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary is more involving than any thriller this year.  这部纪录片比今年任何惊悚片都更扣人心弦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 riveting</td><td>/ˈrɪvɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的，极迷人的</td><td>a riveting account<br>引人入胜的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary offers a riveting account of life inside the mountain villages.  这部纪录片对山村生活的叙述引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dramatic</b></td><td>/drəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>戏剧性的；夸张的；显著的</td><td>a dramatic improvement<br>显著的改善</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her dramatic gestures made the simple announcement seem like a tragedy.  她夸张的手势让这个简单的通知显得像一场悲剧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arresting</td><td>/əˈrestɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的</td><td>an arresting image<br>引人注目的画面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poster used an arresting image of a burning city.  海报用了一幅燃烧城市的醒目画面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bold</td><td>/ˈboʊld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，勇敢的；醒目的</td><td>a bold move<br>大胆的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a bold decision to leave a secure job.  辞去稳定的工作是个大胆的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brilliant</td><td>/ˈbrɪljənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪颖的；辉煌的</td><td>a brilliant student<br>才华出众的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a brilliant mathematician long before she turned twenty.  她还没到二十岁就已是出色的数学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 catchy</td><td>/ˈkætʃi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>棘手的；易记的</td><td>a catchy question<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The examiner slipped a catchy question into the paper.  主考人在试卷里塞了一道棘手的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conspicuous</td><td>/kənˈspɪkjuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>显而易见的；吸引人的</td><td>conspicuous consumption<br>炫耀性消费</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His bright red jacket made him conspicuous in the gray winter crowd.  他鲜红的夹克让他在灰暗的冬日人群中格外显眼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flamboyant</td><td>/flæmˈbɔɪənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>艳丽夺目的，炫耀的，充满装饰的</td><td>a flamboyant style<br>浮夸炫目的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The singer wore a flamboyant coat covered in mirrors and feathers.  歌手穿着一件缀满镜片和羽毛的艳丽夺目的外套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prominent</td><td>/ˈprɑːmənənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>杰出的；显眼的</td><td>a prominent local politician<br>当地知名的政治人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her name appears in a prominent place on the donor list.  她的名字出现在捐赠名单的显眼位置。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remarkable</td><td>/rɪˈmɑːrkəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非凡的，引人注目的</td><td>a remarkable achievement<br>非凡的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Finishing the bridge in a year was a remarkable achievement.  一年建成这座桥是非凡的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 splashy</td><td>/ˈsplæʃi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人注目的，招眼的</td><td>a splashy advertisement<br>一则招眼的广告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magazine ran a splashy cover story about the singer.  杂志刊出一篇关于那位歌手的招眼封面报道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 striking</td><td>/ˈstraɪkɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人注目的，显著的</td><td>a striking resemblance<br>惊人的相似</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a striking contrast between the two sisters' styles.  两姐妹的风格形成鲜明的对比。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discreet</td><td>/dɪˈskriːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言行）谨慎的</td><td>a discreet inquiry<br>谨慎的询问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A discreet inquiry into his background turned up nothing worth reporting.  对他背景的谨慎查访没查出任何值得上报的事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inconspicuous</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈspɪkjuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不显眼的</td><td>remain inconspicuous<br>保持不显眼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He chose an inconspicuous seat at the back of the hall.  他在大厅后排选了个不显眼的座位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 invisible</td><td>/ɪnˈvɪzəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>看不见的，无形的</td><td>invisible to the naked eye<br>肉眼看不见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Radio waves are invisible yet carry sound across continents.  无线电波看不见，却把声音传过大洲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 subtle</td><td>/ˈsʌtl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微妙的，难以感知的</td><td>a subtle difference<br>细微的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a subtle difference between these two shades of blue.  这两种蓝色之间有细微的差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unnoticeable</td><td>/ˌʌnˈnoʊtɪsəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不易察觉的</td><td>almost unnoticeable change<br>几乎察觉不到的变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The repair is so neat it is unnoticeable from a distance.  修补得如此整齐，从远处看根本看不出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unobtrusive</td><td>/ˌʌnəbˈtruːsɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不显眼的，不张扬的</td><td>unobtrusive service<br>不张扬的服务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel's unobtrusive service won it loyal guests.  这家酒店不张扬的服务赢得了忠实客人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>dread</b></td><td>/dred/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恐惧</td><td>fill him with dread<br>使他充满恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thought of another interview filled him with dread all weekend.  一想到还要再面试一次，他整个周末都充满恐惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alarm</td><td>/əˈlɑːrm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>惊恐，恐慌</td><td>cause widespread alarm<br>引起普遍惊恐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden noise filled the whole household with alarm.  突然的响声让全家人惊恐不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apprehension</td><td>/ˌæprɪˈhenʃn/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>忧虑，担心；逮捕</td><td>a sense of apprehension<br>忧虑感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She waited for the results with growing apprehension.  她越来越忧虑地等待结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 consternation</td><td>/ˌkɑːnstərˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>惊愕，惊惶</td><td>to everyone's consternation<br>令所有人惊愕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His resignation caused considerable consternation among the board members.  他的辞职让董事们相当惊愕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fear</td><td>/ˈfɪr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>恐惧，害怕</td><td>fear of failure<br>对失败的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fear of speaking in public gradually faded with practice.  他对公开演讲的恐惧随练习逐渐消退。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fright</td><td>/ˈfraɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>惊恐，恐惧</td><td>give someone a fright<br>让某人吓一跳</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden bang gave the children a terrible fright.  突然的巨响让孩子们大惊失色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horror</td><td>/ˈhɔːrər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恐怖，惊骇</td><td>watch in horror<br>惊骇地看着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers watched in horror as the bridge collapsed.  村民惊骇地看着桥塌了下去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 terror</td><td>/ˈterər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>恐惧，惊骇</td><td>frozen with terror<br>吓得动弹不得</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children ran home in terror after hearing the story.  孩子们听完故事后惊恐地跑回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trepidation</td><td>/ˌtrepɪˈdeɪʃn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恐惧，战栗</td><td>with some trepidation<br>怀着几分恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She opened the letter with obvious trepidation, fearing the worst news.  她带着明显的恐惧打开信，担心是最坏的消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bravery</td><td>/ˈbreɪvəri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇敢，英勇</td><td>an act of bravery<br>勇敢之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was given a medal for bravery during the flood.  她因在洪水中的勇敢而获得奖章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 courage</td><td>/ˈkɜːrədʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>勇气，胆量</td><td>summon up courage<br>鼓起勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took real courage to admit the mistake in front of everyone.  在众人面前承认错误需要真正的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 dauntlessness</del></td><td>/ˈdɔːntləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无畏，大胆</td><td>the dauntlessness of youth<br>年轻人的无畏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His dauntlessness in the storm saved the whole crew.  他在风暴中的无畏救了全体船员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fearlessness</td><td>/ˈfɪrləsnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无畏，大胆</td><td>admired for her fearlessness<br>因无畏而受敬佩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her fearlessness in the courtroom impressed even hostile judges.  她在法庭上的无畏甚至让敌意的法官动容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fortitude</td><td>/ˈfɔːrtɪˌtuːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>坚韧，刚毅</td><td>bear pain with fortitude<br>坚韧地忍受痛苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She bore months of treatment with quiet fortitude.  她默默坚韧地承受了数月的治疗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intrepidity</td><td>/ˌɪntrəˈpɪdəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇敢，无畏</td><td>show great intrepidity<br>表现出极大勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot's intrepidity saved everyone aboard that night.  那晚飞行员的无畏救了机上所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 stoutness</del></td><td>/ˈstaʊtnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>刚毅，勇敢坚定</td><td>stoutness of heart<br>心志刚毅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers praised his stoutness during the long, hopeless siege.  村民赞扬他在漫长绝望的围城中的刚毅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 valor</td><td>/ˈvælər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>英勇，勇猛</td><td>an award for valor<br>英勇奖章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He received a medal for valor after rescuing two trapped miners.  他救出两名被困矿工后获得英勇勋章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>duplicate</b></td><td>/ˈduːpləkət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>复制品</td><td>an exact duplicate<br>完全一样的副本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Keep an exact duplicate of the contract in a separate locked drawer.  把合同的一份完全一样的副本另放在一个上锁的抽屉里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 copy</td><td>/ˈkɑːpi/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>复制品，仿制品；抄写</td><td>a cheap copy<br>廉价仿制品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting on the wall is only a cheap copy.  墙上那幅画只是廉价的仿制品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dupe</td><td>/duːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>dupe trusting investors<br>欺骗轻信的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scheme duped trusting investors out of nearly a million dollars.  这个骗局欺骗轻信的投资者，骗走近百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 duplication</td><td>/ˌduːpləˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制品，重复</td><td>an exact duplication<br>精确的复制</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting is a faithful duplication of a lost original.  这幅画是失传原作的忠实复制。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 facsimile</td><td>/fækˈsɪməli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制品，摹本</td><td>a perfect facsimile<br>完美的摹本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum shows a facsimile while the original stays hidden.  博物馆展出摹本，原件则藏而不露。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imitation</td><td>/ˌɪmɪˈteɪʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>仿制品</td><td>a cheap imitation<br>廉价的仿制品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bag was a cheap imitation sold openly at the night market.  那只包是夜市上公开出售的廉价仿制品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mock</td><td>/ˈmɑːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>假的，模拟的；嘲笑</td><td>a mock trial<br>模拟审判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students held a mock election to learn how voting works.  学生举行模拟选举以了解投票如何运作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reduplication</td><td>/riˌdupləˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制，重复</td><td>endless reduplication of forms<br>表格的无尽重复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office suffers from constant reduplication of the same paperwork.  这个办公室饱受同样文书不断重复之苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 replica</td><td>/ˈreplɪkə/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制品，仿制品</td><td>a full-size replica<br>原尺寸的复制品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Visitors climb a wooden replica of the ancient tower.  游客可以爬上那座古塔的木制复制品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 replication</td><td>/ˌrepləˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制品；复制</td><td>a faithful replication<br>忠实的复制品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum displays a careful replication of the ancient bronze vase.  博物馆展出了那只古代青铜花瓶的精细复制品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reproduction</td><td>/ˌriːprəˈdʌkʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制品；复制</td><td>a cheap reproduction<br>廉价的复制品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He hung a reproduction of the famous painting above his desk.  他在书桌上方挂了那幅名画的复制品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clone</td><td>/ˈkloʊn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复制品，翻版</td><td>a cheap clone<br>廉价的翻版</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new phone is basically a clone of last year's model.  这款新手机基本上是去年型号的翻版。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 copycat</td><td>/ˈkɑːpiˌkæt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>盲目模仿者，抄袭者</td><td>a copycat crime<br>模仿性的犯罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magazine was a shameless copycat of a French title.  这本杂志无耻地照抄了一份法国刊物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imitate</td><td>/ˈɪməˌteɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>模仿，仿效</td><td>imitate his accent<br>模仿他的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children naturally imitate the speech of adults around them.  孩子会自然地模仿身边大人的说话方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 reduplicate</del></td><td>/riˈduːplɪkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>重复，加倍复制</td><td>reduplicate the same error<br>重复同样的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Later editions merely reduplicated the printer's earlier mistakes.  后来的版本只是重复了印工先前的错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 render</td><td>/ˈrendər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>交出，给予；使成为</td><td>render assistance<br>提供援助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The neighbors rendered assistance long before the ambulance arrived.  邻居们在救护车到达前很久就提供了援助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 replicate</td><td>/ˈreplɪkeɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>复制；重复（实验）</td><td>replicate the experiment exactly<br>精确重复该实验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two other laboratories failed to replicate the results of the original experiment.  另外两个实验室未能重复出原实验的结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reproduce</td><td>/ˌriːprəˈduːs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>复制，再现；繁殖</td><td>reproduce the painting faithfully<br>忠实复制这幅画</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The catalog reproduces every drawing in full color.  图录以全彩复制了每一幅素描。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 archetype</td><td>/ˈɑːrkɪtaɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>原型，典型</td><td>the archetype of a hero<br>英雄的典型</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sherlock Holmes remains the archetype of the brilliant private detective.  夏洛克·福尔摩斯仍是天才私家侦探的原型。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 original</td><td>/əˈrɪdʒənl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新的；最初的，最早的</td><td>an original idea<br>有创意的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her proposal was genuinely original, unlike the tired ideas we usually hear.  她的提案确实新颖，不像我们常听到的那些陈旧想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prototype</td><td>/ˈproʊtətaɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>原型</td><td>a working prototype<br>可运行的原型</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers tested a working prototype of the electric bus last spring.  工程师去年春天测试了这款电动公交车的可用原型。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>eclectic</b></td><td>/ɪˈklektɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>博采众长的；不拘一格的</td><td>an eclectic collection<br>混合多元的收藏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her taste in music is remarkably eclectic and hard to label.  她的音乐品味相当博杂，难以归类。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assorted</td><td>/əˈsɔːrtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>各式各样的，混合的</td><td>assorted chocolates<br>什锦巧克力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She brought a box of assorted chocolates to the office.  她带了一盒什锦巧克力到办公室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diverse</td><td>/daɪˈvɜːrs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不同的，多样的</td><td>diverse backgrounds<br>各不相同的背景</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The class brings together students from diverse cultural backgrounds.  这个班汇集了来自不同文化背景的学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heterogeneous</td><td>/ˌhetərəˈdʒiːniəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>异质的，混杂的</td><td>a heterogeneous mixture<br>混杂的混合物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The class is a heterogeneous mixture of ages and backgrounds.  这个班是年龄和背景的混杂组合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indiscriminate</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈskrɪmɪnət/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不加区别的，随意的</td><td>indiscriminate shelling<br>无差别炮击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The indiscriminate use of pesticides harmed local bees.  杀虫剂的滥用伤害了当地蜜蜂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magpie</td><td>/ˈmæɡˌpaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>喜鹊；爱收集杂物者</td><td>a magpie collection<br>杂七杂八的收藏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His study is a magpie hoard of maps, coins and shells.  他的书房是地图、硬币和贝壳的杂堆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mixed</td><td>/ˈmɪkst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混合的，掺杂的；好坏交织的</td><td>mixed feelings<br>复杂的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I have mixed feelings about moving abroad.  对于出国我心情复杂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 motley</td><td>/ˈmɑːtli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>混杂的，富于变化的</td><td>a motley crowd<br>混杂的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A motley crowd of farmers, students, and shopkeepers filled the town square.  农民、学生和店主组成的混杂人群挤满了市镇广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 piebald</td><td>/ˈpaɪbɔːld/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有黑白斑块的</td><td>a piebald horse<br>黑白花的马</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A piebald horse grazed quietly beside the wooden fence.  一匹黑白花的马在木栅栏旁静静吃草。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ragtag</td><td>/ˈræɡˌtæɡ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>杂凑的，混杂的</td><td>a ragtag group of volunteers<br>一群杂凑的志愿者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A ragtag crew of amateurs somehow finished the whole film.  一伙杂凑的业余人员居然拍完了整部电影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 varied</td><td>/ˈverid/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>各式各样的，多变的</td><td>a varied diet<br>多样的饮食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A varied diet keeps both cook and eater interested.  多样的饮食让做饭的和吃饭的都有兴致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 homogeneous</td><td>/ˌhoʊməˈdʒiːniəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>同质的，同类的</td><td>a homogeneous population<br>同质的人口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The town's population is unusually homogeneous in language and faith.  该镇人口在语言和信仰上异常同质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uniform</td><td>/ˈjuːnəˌfɔːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>一致的，统一的</td><td>a uniform standard<br>统一的标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bricks were baked to a uniform shade of red.  这些砖被烧成统一的红色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>efficacious</b></td><td>/ˌefɪˈkeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有效的</td><td>an efficacious treatment<br>有效的治疗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old remedy proved surprisingly efficacious against mild seasonal coughs.  这个老方子治轻微的季节性咳嗽出乎意料地有效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 effective</td><td>/ɪˈfektɪv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有效的，起作用的</td><td>an effective solution<br>有效的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simple hand washing is an effective way to prevent illness.  简单洗手是预防疾病的有效方法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 effectual</td><td>/ɪˈfɛktʃuəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有效的，起作用的</td><td>an effectual remedy<br>有效的疗法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simple hand washing proved the most effectual precaution available.  简单的洗手被证明是最有效的预防措施。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 efficient</td><td>/ɪˈfɪʃənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>高效的，有效率的</td><td>an efficient filing system<br>高效的归档系统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new software makes the whole process far more efficient.  新软件让整个流程效率高得多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fruitful</td><td>/ˈfruːtfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>富有成果的，多产的</td><td>a fruitful discussion<br>富有成果的讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their collaboration proved fruitful and produced several important papers.  他们的合作富有成果，产出了几篇重要论文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 operative</td><td>/ˈɑːpərətɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>有效的，起作用的</td><td>the operative rule here<br>此处有效的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The older statute is no longer operative in this state.  那部旧法规在本州已不再有效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 potent</td><td>/ˈpoʊtənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强有力的，有效力的</td><td>a potent argument<br>有力的论据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fear of shame is a potent motive in small communities.  在小社群中，怕丢脸是一种强有力的动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 productive</td><td>/prəˈdʌktɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多产的，富有成效的</td><td>a productive meeting<br>富有成效的会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The morning was productive; we finished three chapters before lunch.  上午很有成效，午饭前我们写完了三章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 bootless</del></td><td>/ˈbuːtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无用的</td><td>a bootless effort<br>无用的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arguing with him about money proved a bootless effort.  和他争论钱的事结果是无用的努力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fruitless</td><td>/ˈfruːtləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无成效的，白费的</td><td>a fruitless search<br>毫无结果的搜寻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Weeks of fruitless negotiation left both sides exhausted.  数周毫无结果的谈判让双方都疲惫不堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ineffective</td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈfektɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无效的，不起作用的</td><td>an ineffective treatment<br>无效的治疗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old medicine proved completely ineffective against the new virus.  这种旧药对新病毒完全无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ineffectual</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈfektʃuəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无效的，徒劳的</td><td>an ineffectual protest<br>徒劳的抗议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ineffectual attempts to calm the crowd only made things worse.  他徒劳的安抚只让人群更加激动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inefficient</td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈfɪʃənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>效率低的，浪费的</td><td>an inefficient old engine<br>效率低下的旧发动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heating system is old, noisy, and terribly inefficient.  这套供暖系统老旧、吵闹，而且效率极低。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inoperative</td><td>/ɪnˈɑːpərətɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不起作用的，失效的</td><td>an inoperative rule<br>已失效的规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old safety clause in the contract is now inoperative.  合同里那条旧的安全条款现已失效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unproductive</td><td>/ˌʌnprəˈdʌktɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无成效的</td><td>unproductive rocky soil<br>贫瘠的石质土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of drought left the fields dry and unproductive.  多年干旱使这些田地干枯贫瘠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 useless</td><td>/ˈjuːsləs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无用的，无效的</td><td>practically useless<br>几乎毫无用处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Without a charger the expensive phone was completely useless.  没有充电器，这部贵手机完全没用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>elude</b></td><td>/ɪˈluːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>躲闪，躲避；使无法理解，使困惑</td><td>elude the police<br>躲开警察</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief eluded the police for nearly six months before being caught.  小偷躲开警方近六个月才被抓住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avoid</td><td>/əˈvɔɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避免，回避</td><td>avoid the issue<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He carefully avoided answering the reporter's most difficult question.  他小心地避开了记者最难的那个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dodge</td><td>/dɑːdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>躲避</td><td>dodge the question<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister managed to dodge the question three times in one interview.  部长在一次采访中三次成功回避了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 duck</td><td>/dʌk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>躲避，回避；迅速低头</td><td>duck the question<br>回避这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister ducked every question about the budget.  部长回避了所有关于预算的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eschew</td><td>/ɪsˈtʃuː/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刻意避开；戒绝</td><td>eschew violence<br>戒绝暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monks eschew luxury and live on whatever the village offers.  僧侣们避开奢华，靠村民所给的一切生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evade</td><td>/ɪˈveɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>逃避，规避</td><td>evade the question<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness evaded every question about the missing money.  证人回避了所有关于失踪钱款的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 finesse</td><td>/fɪˈnes/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>娴熟技巧；（巧妙地）躲避</td><td>handle it with finesse<br>娴熟巧妙地处理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She handled the awkward question with real finesse and everyone relaxed.  她娴熟巧妙地处理了那个尴尬的问题，大家都放松了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scape</td><td>/ˈskeɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>逃脱，避开</td><td>scape the worst of it<br>躲过最糟的部分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village scaped the worst of the flood by sheer luck.  这个村子纯凭运气躲过了洪水最猛的部分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shirk</td><td>/ʃɜːrk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>逃避，规避</td><td>shirk responsibility<br>逃避责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one on this team is allowed to shirk hard work.  这个团队里没人可以逃避苦活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shun</td><td>/ʃʌn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>避开，避免</td><td>shun public attention<br>避开公众关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The writer chose to shun public attention after winning the prize.  那位作家获奖后选择避开公众关注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sidestep</td><td>/ˈsaɪdstep/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>回避，避开（问题）</td><td>sidestep the issue<br>回避这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidate neatly sidestepped the issue of rising unemployment.  这位候选人巧妙地回避了失业率上升的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weasel</td><td>/ˈwiːzəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>耍滑逃避（责任）；黄鼠狼</td><td>weasel out of a promise<br>耍滑推掉承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to weasel out of paying his share.  他试图耍滑不付自己那份钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baffle</td><td>/ˈbæfl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使疑惑</td><td>baffle the experts<br>使专家困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange symptoms continued to baffle the visiting experts.  这些奇怪的症状一直使来访的专家疑惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 befog</del></td><td>/bɪˈfɔːɡ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使模糊，使困惑</td><td>befog the argument<br>使论证变模糊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Technical jargon befogs the argument instead of making it clearer.  技术术语让论证更模糊，而非更清楚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 befuddle</del></td><td>/bɪˈfʌdl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使困惑</td><td>befuddle the audience<br>使听众困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long tables of statistics only befuddle the general audience.  长长的统计表格只会使普通听众困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bewilder</td><td>/bɪˈwɪldər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使迷惑，使不知所措</td><td>bewilder the newcomers<br>使新来者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The maze of corridors bewildered every visitor on the first day.  迷宫般的走廊让每位来访者第一天都不知所措。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confound</td><td>/kənˈfaʊnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑；证明为假，证伪</td><td>confound the critics<br>让批评者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The late victory confounded the critics who had predicted failure.  最后的胜利让预言失败的批评者困惑不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 discombobulate</del></td><td>/ˌdɪskəmˈbɑːbjuleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使迷惑，使慌乱</td><td>discombobulate the speaker<br>使演讲者慌乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unexpected question completely discombobulated the young candidate.  这个意外的问题让年轻候选人彻底慌了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disorient</td><td>/dɪsˈɔːrient/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使迷失方向，使困惑</td><td>disorient the visitors<br>使来访者迷向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thick fog disoriented the hikers within a few minutes.  浓雾几分钟内就让徒步者失去了方向感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maze</td><td>/ˈmeɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>迷宫；错综复杂</td><td>a maze of corridors<br>迷宫般的走廊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We got lost in a maze of narrow hospital corridors.  我们在迷宫般的狭窄医院走廊里迷了路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mystify</td><td>/ˈmɪstəˌfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑，使迷惑</td><td>mystify the experts<br>使专家们困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden failure of the engine mystified the mechanics.  发动机的突然故障让机械师们十分困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perplex</td><td>/pərˈpleks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑，使费解</td><td>perplex the researchers<br>使研究者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange results perplexed the researchers for several long months.  奇怪的结果让研究者困惑了好几个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 puzzle</td><td>/ˈpʌzəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使困惑；谜题</td><td>puzzle the doctors<br>使医生们困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sudden recovery puzzled the doctors who had treated her.  她的突然康复让治疗过她的医生们感到困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vex</td><td>/veks/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使烦恼，使恼怒</td><td>vex the teacher<br>使老师恼怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant interruptions vexed the teacher throughout the long afternoon lesson.  不断的打扰让老师在漫长的下午课上十分恼怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 confront</td><td>/kənˈfrʌnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>直接对抗，直面</td><td>confront the problem<br>直面问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sooner or later you must confront the problem directly.  你早晚必须直面这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clarify</td><td>/ˈklerɪfaɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阐明，澄清</td><td>clarify the rules<br>澄清规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dean clarified the rules after weeks of confused complaints.  在数周混乱的投诉后，院长澄清了规则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 elucidate</td><td>/iˈluːsɪdeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>阐明</td><td>elucidate the problem<br>阐明问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The footnotes elucidate the problem far better than the chapter itself does.  脚注比正文本身更好地阐明了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 explicate</td><td>/ˈeksplɪkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解释，说明</td><td>explicate a difficult text<br>解说一段难懂的文本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher patiently explicated the most difficult passage in the poem.  老师耐心地解说了这首诗中最难的一段。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expound</td><td>/ɪkˈspaʊnd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>详细阐述，解释</td><td>expound on his theory<br>详细阐述他的理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor expounded her views on urban design for an hour.  教授用一个小时详细阐述了她的城市设计观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 illuminate</td><td>/ɪˈluːmɪneɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阐明</td><td>illuminate the problem<br>阐明问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lecture illuminated a problem that had puzzled economists for years.  她的讲座阐明了一个困扰经济学家多年的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 illustrate</td><td>/ˈɪləsˌtreɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>说明，举例说明</td><td>illustrate the point<br>说明这一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These figures illustrate how quickly the population has grown.  这些数字说明人口增长得多么迅速。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>enamored</b></td><td>/ɪˈnæmərd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷恋的，热爱的</td><td>enamored of her voice<br>迷恋她的嗓音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was so enamored of her voice that he attended every concert.  他如此迷恋她的嗓音，以致每场音乐会都去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 besotted</td><td>/bɪˈsɑːtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷恋的，痴迷的</td><td>besotted with the newcomer<br>迷恋那位新来者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was utterly besotted with her from the first evening.  从第一晚起他就彻底迷上了她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bewitched</td><td>/bɪˈwɪtʃt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>被迷住的，着魔的</td><td>bewitched by the music<br>被音乐迷住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The audience sat bewitched by her quiet, steady voice.  观众被她平静沉稳的嗓音迷住，静坐不动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 captivated</td><td>/ˈkæptɪˌveɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>被迷住的，倾心的</td><td>captivated by the story<br>被故事迷住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Readers were captivated by the novel's strange opening chapter.  读者被这部小说奇特的开篇迷住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crazy</td><td>/ˈkreɪzi/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>疯狂的，荒唐的</td><td>a crazy idea<br>疯狂的主意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It sounded like a crazy idea until it actually worked.  这主意听着很疯，直到它真的成功了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dotty</td><td>/ˈdɑːti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>痴迷的；有点糊涂的</td><td>dotty about old films<br>痴迷老电影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My uncle is absolutely dotty about vintage motorcycles.  我叔叔对老式摩托车痴迷得不行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enraptured</td><td>/enˈræptʃərd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>欣喜若狂的，着迷的</td><td>an enraptured audience<br>如痴如醉的观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The enraptured audience refused to leave after three encores.  如痴如醉的观众在三次返场后仍不肯走。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fascinated</td><td>/ˈfæsəˌneɪtəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>着迷的，入迷的</td><td>fascinated by insects<br>对昆虫着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has been fascinated by deep-sea creatures since childhood.  她从小就对深海生物着迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infatuated</td><td>/ɪnˈfætʃueɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷恋的，痴恋的</td><td>infatuated with a classmate<br>迷恋一位同学</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boy was hopelessly infatuated with his piano teacher.  这男孩无可救药地迷恋他的钢琴老师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mad</td><td>/ˈmæd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>疯狂的，荒唐的；愤怒的</td><td>a mad scheme<br>荒唐的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be mad to sell the house at that price.  以那个价格卖房子简直荒唐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nuts</td><td>/ˈnʌts/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>痴迷的；发疯的</td><td>nuts about baseball<br>对棒球着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone in that family is completely nuts about gardening.  那家人个个对园艺痴迷得很。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obsessed</td><td>/əbˈsest/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>着迷的，念念不忘的</td><td>obsessed with tidiness<br>对整洁近乎着魔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grew obsessed with solving that one old puzzle.  他变得一心只想解开那个老谜题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apathetic</td><td>/ˌæpəˈθetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷漠的，无兴趣的</td><td>apathetic voters<br>冷漠的选民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most students seemed apathetic about the election and did not vote.  大多数学生对这次选举漠不关心，也没有投票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 detached</td><td>/dɪˈtætʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷漠超然的，不介入的</td><td>a detached attitude<br>超然冷漠的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She described the accident in a strangely detached voice.  她用一种异常冷漠的语气描述了那场事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indifferent</td><td>/ɪnˈdɪfrənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不感兴趣的，冷漠的；平庸的</td><td>indifferent to criticism<br>对批评漠不关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is entirely indifferent to what her critics say.  她对批评者说什么完全无所谓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insouciant</td><td>/ɪnˈsuːsiənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无忧虑的，不在乎的</td><td>an insouciant shrug<br>满不在乎地耸肩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the harsh criticism with an insouciant shrug and a smile.  他对严厉的批评只是满不在乎地耸耸肩，笑了笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nonchalant</td><td>/ˌnɑːnʃəˈlɑːnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>漫不经心的，淡然的</td><td>a nonchalant shrug<br>漫不经心地一耸肩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the accusation with a nonchalant shrug and changed the subject.  他冷淡地一耸肩回应指控，然后换了话题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>endeavor</b></td><td>/ɪnˈdevər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>努力，奋斗</td><td>a joint scientific endeavor<br>共同的科学努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mission was a joint scientific endeavor involving eleven different countries.  这次任务是一项有十一个国家参与的共同科学努力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attempt</td><td>/əˈtempt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>尝试，努力</td><td>a bold rescue attempt<br>大胆的营救尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her third attempt at the summit finally succeeded.  她第三次尝试登顶终于成功了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 essay</td><td>/eˈseɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>尝试，试图</td><td>essay a difficult task<br>尝试一项难事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He essayed a smile, but his hands were shaking.  他勉力一笑，可双手还在抖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 striving</td><td>/ˈstraɪvɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>努力，奋斗</td><td>constant striving for perfection<br>对完美的不断追求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lifelong striving for accuracy shaped the entire field.  她一生对精确的追求塑造了整个领域。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 struggle</td><td>/ˈstrʌɡəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>挣扎，费力地做；斗争</td><td>struggle to keep up<br>挣扎着跟上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He struggled to keep up with the rest of the class.  他挣扎着跟上班上其他同学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trial</td><td>/ˈtraɪəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>试验；审判</td><td>a clinical trial<br>一项临床试验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The drug performed well in a small clinical trial.  该药在一项小型临床试验中表现良好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 undertaking</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈteɪkɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>任务，事业</td><td>a huge undertaking<br>一项艰巨的事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Restoring the cathedral was an enormous undertaking.  修复这座大教堂是一项浩大的工程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assay</td><td>/æˈseɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>尝试，试图；化验</td><td>assay a new approach<br>尝试新办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young climber assayed the north face alone.  这位年轻登山者独自尝试了北壁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drudge</td><td>/ˈdrʌdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>做苦工的人；做苦工</td><td>work like a drudge<br>像苦工一样干活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She drudged in the kitchen from dawn to midnight.  她从清晨到午夜在厨房里做苦工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hustle</td><td>/ˈhʌsəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>催促；奋力行进</td><td>hustle through the crowd<br>在人群中挤行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She hustled the children into the car before the rain.  她在下雨前把孩子们匆匆赶上车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 moil</del></td><td>/mɔɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>骚动，混乱；苦干</td><td>the moil of the city<br>城市的喧扰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He escaped the moil of the city for a quiet island.  他逃离城市的喧扰，去了个安静的小岛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plod</td><td>/ˈplɑːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>沉重缓慢地走，苦干</td><td>plod through the mud<br>在泥地里沉重跋涉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tired horses plodded through the deep mud.  疲惫的马匹在深泥中沉重跋涉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slave</td><td>/ˈsleɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>苦干，拼命工作</td><td>slave away all night<br>整夜苦干</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She slaved over the manuscript for eleven straight months.  她为这部书稿连续苦干了十一个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strain</td><td>/ˈstreɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>竭力，尽力</td><td>strain to hear the words<br>竭力想听清话语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He strained to lift the crate onto the truck.  他竭力把箱子抬上卡车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strive</td><td>/ˈstraɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>努力，奋斗</td><td>strive for a better life<br>为更好的生活奋斗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They strive daily to keep the small clinic running.  他们每天都在努力维持这家小诊所的运转。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sweat</td><td>/ˈswet/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>苦活；出汗</td><td>no sweat at all<br>毫不费力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Moving those crates was a real sweat.  搬那些箱子真是件苦活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seek</td><td>/ˈsiːk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>寻求，追求</td><td>seek professional advice<br>寻求专业建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many families seek shelter in the school gym during heavy storms.  暴风雨期间许多家庭到学校体育馆寻求庇护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 toil</td><td>/ˈtɔɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>辛苦劳作，苦干</td><td>toil in the fields<br>在田里劳作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farmers toiled from sunrise until dark during harvest.  收获时农民从日出劳作到天黑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 travail</td><td>/ˈtræveɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>辛苦劳动</td><td>years of travail<br>多年辛劳</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After months of travail, the small team finally finished the bridge.  经过数月的辛苦劳作，这个小团队终于建成了这座桥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tug</td><td>/ˈtʌɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>用力拉；奋力</td><td>tug at the heavy rope<br>用力拉粗绳</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child tugged at his mother's sleeve impatiently.  孩子不耐烦地拉着母亲的袖子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 drop</td><td>/ˈdrɑːp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>下降，减少；落下</td><td>drop sharply<br>急剧下降</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sales dropped by half after the price increase.  涨价后销量下降了一半。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quit</td><td>/ˈkwɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>退出，辞去；停止</td><td>quit the organization<br>退出该组织</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She quit the committee after the second disagreement.  第二次分歧之后她退出了委员会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>enthrall</b></td><td>/ɪnˈθrɔːl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（像用魔咒般）吸引</td><td>enthrall the entire audience<br>吸引住全场观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician enthralled the entire audience with a single deck of cards.  魔术师仅用一副牌就吸引住了全场观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 absorb</td><td>/əbˈzɔːrb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使全神贯注；吸收</td><td>be absorbed in the book<br>沉浸在书里</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puzzle absorbed him completely for the whole afternoon.  这道谜题让他整个下午都全神贯注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arrest</td><td>/əˈrest/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>停止；逮捕</td><td>place him under arrest<br>将他逮捕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new drug can arrest the spread of the disease for years.  这种新药能在数年内阻止疾病扩散。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bedazzle</td><td>/bɪˈdæzəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使眼花缭乱，使着迷</td><td>bedazzle the audience<br>使观众目眩神迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The acrobats bedazzled the crowd with almost impossible leaps.  杂技演员用几乎不可能的腾跃让观众目眩神迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enchant</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃænt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使入迷，使陶醉</td><td>be enchanted by the scenery<br>被风景迷住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old fishing village enchanted every visitor who stayed there.  那个古老渔村令每位住过的游客陶醉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 engross</td><td>/ɪnˈɡroʊs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使全神贯注</td><td>engross the young reader<br>使小读者全神贯注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The adventure novel engrossed the young reader for an entire rainy weekend.  这本冒险小说让小读者整个雨天的周末都全神贯注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fascinate</td><td>/ˈfæsɪneɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈吸引，使入迷</td><td>fascinate young readers<br>使小读者着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stories about strange deep-sea creatures fascinate young readers all over the world.  关于奇异深海生物的故事让全世界的小读者着迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grip</td><td>/ˈɡrɪp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>吸引住；紧握</td><td>grip the reader's attention<br>抓住读者注意力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film gripped us from the very first scene.  这部电影从第一个镜头就抓住了我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hypnotize</td><td>/ˈhɪpnəˌtaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使着迷；对…施催眠术</td><td>hypnotize the audience<br>使观众入迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancer's slow movements seemed to hypnotize everyone watching.  舞者缓慢的动作似乎让所有观看者入迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immerse</td><td>/ɪˈmɜːrs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沉浸；浸入</td><td>immerse herself in research<br>使自己沉浸于研究</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He immersed himself in the language by living with a local family.  他住在当地人家中，让自己沉浸在语言环境里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mesmerize</td><td>/ˈmezməraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使入迷，迷住</td><td>mesmerize the whole crowd<br>迷住全场人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The flickering firelight mesmerized the tired children and kept them quiet.  跳动的火光让疲倦的孩子们看得入迷，安静了下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 spellbind</del></td><td>/ˈspɛlbaɪnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>迷住，使入迷</td><td>spellbind the whole audience<br>迷住全场观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her stories could spellbind a room full of restless children.  她的故事能让满屋好动的孩子入迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bore</td><td>/bɔːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>令人厌烦的人或事物</td><td>an unbearable old bore<br>让人受不了的老唠叨鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My uncle is such a bore that guests leave early.  我叔叔特别无趣，客人们都早早离席。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 jade</td><td>/ˈdʒeɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使厌倦，使疲惫；玉</td><td>jaded by routine<br>被日常琐事弄厌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of endless touring had jaded the young singer.  多年不断的巡演使这位年轻歌手厌倦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pall</td><td>/pɔːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>令人厌倦，失去吸引力</td><td>begin to pall<br>开始令人生厌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novelty of city life began to pall after his first lonely winter.  在孤独的第一个冬天过后，城市生活的新鲜感开始让他生厌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tire</td><td>/ˈtaɪər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使疲劳，感到疲倦</td><td>tire easily<br>容易疲倦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long meetings tire her far more than a hard morning run.  长会比清晨的剧烈跑步更让她疲倦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weary</td><td>/ˈwɪri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>令人厌倦的；疲倦的</td><td>a weary routine<br>令人厌倦的日常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The weary meetings dragged on with no decision in sight.  这些令人厌倦的会议一拖再拖，看不到任何决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>equilibrium</b></td><td>/ˌiːkwɪˈlɪbriəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>均势，平衡</td><td>reach a stable equilibrium<br>达到稳定的平衡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market took two years to reach a stable equilibrium again.  市场花了两年才重新达到稳定的平衡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 counterpoise</del></td><td>/ˈkaʊntərˌpɔɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使平衡，抗衡；平衡力</td><td>counterpoise two forces<br>使两种力量抗衡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senate was designed to counterpoise the power of the president.  参议院的设计意在抗衡总统的权力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equilibration</td><td>/iˌkwɪlɪˈbreɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平衡，均衡作用</td><td>the equilibration of pressure<br>压力的平衡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Slow equilibration of temperature took nearly two hours.  温度缓慢达到平衡花了将近两小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equipoise</td><td>/ˈekwəpɔɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平衡，均势</td><td>hold in equipoise<br>保持均势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two powers stayed in uneasy equipoise for a decade.  两大势力维持了十年不安的均势。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 poise</td><td>/ˈpɔɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>平衡；沉着</td><td>keep her poise<br>保持沉着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered the hostile questions with remarkable poise.  她以非凡的沉着回答了那些带敌意的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stasis</td><td>/ˈsteɪsɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>停滞，静止状态</td><td>a period of stasis<br>一段停滞时期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The industry entered a long stasis after the financial crisis.  金融危机后该行业进入长期停滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 disequilibration</del></td><td>/ˌdɪsɪkwɪlɪˈbreɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>失衡，不平衡作用</td><td>sudden market disequilibration<br>市场骤然失衡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The drug caused temporary disequilibration in the inner ear.  这种药导致内耳暂时失衡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disequilibrium</td><td>/dɪˌsiːkwəˈlɪbriəm/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>失衡，不平衡状态</td><td>economic disequilibrium<br>经济失衡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of cheap credit produced a dangerous disequilibrium.  多年的廉价信贷造成了危险的失衡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imbalance</td><td>/ɪmˈbæləns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>不平衡，失衡</td><td>a serious trade imbalance<br>严重的贸易失衡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The trade imbalance between the two countries widened again last year.  两国之间的贸易失衡去年再次扩大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nonequilibrium</td><td>/ˌnɑnikwɪˈlɪbriəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>非平衡（状态）</td><td>a nonequilibrium system<br>非平衡系统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Living cells maintain a nonequilibrium steady state far from equilibrium.  活细胞维持着远离平衡的非平衡稳态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unbalance</td><td>/ʌnˈbæləns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使失衡；不平衡</td><td>unbalance the whole system<br>使整个系统失衡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One heavy passenger can unbalance a small canoe.  一名重乘客就能让小独木舟失衡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>escort</b></td><td>/ɪˈskɔːrt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>同行，护送</td><td>escort the guests out<br>送客人出去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two guards escorted the visiting minister to the waiting car.  两名警卫护送来访的部长上了等候的汽车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accompany</td><td>/əˈkʌmpəni/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>陪同，伴随</td><td>accompany her home<br>陪她回家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered to accompany her home after the concert.  音乐会结束后他提出陪她回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attend</td><td>/əˈtend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>倾听，注意</td><td>attend to his words<br>留意他的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please attend carefully to what the doctor tells you.  请仔细听医生对你说的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chaperone</td><td>/ˈʃæpəroʊn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>同行，护送</td><td>chaperone the students<br>陪同学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two parents chaperoned the students on the museum trip.  两位家长陪同学生去博物馆参观。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 companion</td><td>/kəmˈpænjən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>同伴，陪伴者</td><td>a travelling companion<br>旅伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found a cheerful travelling companion on the night train.  她在夜车上遇到一位快活的旅伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 company</td><td>/ˈkʌmpəni/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陪伴，同伴；公司</td><td>keep bad company<br>结交坏朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His parents worried that he was keeping bad company at school.  他父母担心他在学校结交坏朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 convoy</td><td>/ˈkɑːnˌvɔɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>护送，护航；车队</td><td>convoy the supplies<br>护送物资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warships convoyed the supplies across the open sea.  军舰护送物资横渡公海。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squire</td><td>/ˈskwaɪər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>陪伴（女士）；乡绅</td><td>squire a lady to dinner<br>陪女士赴宴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He squired his elderly aunt to the village dance.  他陪年长的姑妈去村里的舞会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abandon</td><td>/əˈbændən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>放纵，尽情；放弃</td><td>with reckless abandon<br>肆意放纵地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She danced with wild abandon at the wedding reception.  她在婚宴上尽情忘我地跳舞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 desert</td><td>/dɪˈzɜːrt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抛弃，遗弃</td><td>desert one's post<br>擅离职守</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain refused to desert his crew in the storm.  船长拒绝在风暴中抛下船员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ditch</td><td>/ˈdɪtʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>抛弃，摆脱；沟渠</td><td>ditch the old plan<br>放弃旧计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team ditched the old design after two failed tests.  两次测试失败后，团队放弃了旧设计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
